# pixi-silk: text, layout, UI widgets and the editor kit

> Smooth vector graphics for PixiJS v8. Every shape is an exact signed distance field, anti-aliased per pixel in the shader, so edges stay smooth at any zoom, rotation and device pixel ratio without MSAA.

pixi-silk 0.1.0 is an MIT-licensed TypeScript library. It needs pixi.js ^8.6 as a peer dependency, and a browser with WebGL2 or WebGPU.

Site: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev
Repository: https://github.com/schmooky/pixi-silk
Package: https://www.npmjs.com/package/pixi-silk

This file holds the guides on text, layout, UI widgets and the editor kit, with the source of their live demos, then the API of their entry points. Each lives under its own import path.

- [llms-full.txt](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/llms-full.txt): core, paint and rendering: guides and API, SilkGraphics first.
- [llms-examples.txt](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/llms-examples.txt): whole screens with the source of every demo.

## Gotchas

- WebGL2 is the default renderer. For WebGPU pass `createSilkApp({ preference: 'webgpu' })`, or in your own Pixi app call `enableWebGPU()` from `pixi-silk/webgpu` before you create the first SilkGraphics.
- Stroke joins are `miter` by default, as in Canvas 2D and Pixi. `bevel` and `round` are the others. `miterLimit` defaults to 10.
- Free-form paths (`moveTo`, `lineTo`, Bezier curves, `poly`, `svgPath`) both fill and stroke. A fill paints a path as one exact even-odd shape.
- Shape operations: `fill({ op: 'union' | 'subtract' | 'intersect' | 'exclude' })` on the shapes of the active path, `g.boolean(op, a, b)` to nest them, and `g.svgPath(d)` for SVG path data.
- Batching is off by default: each SilkGraphics is one draw call. Call `enableSilkBatching()` before you create the objects to batch runs of them, on WebGL2 and WebGPU. `{ batch: false }` keeps one object out.
- Each primitive is one instance of 44 floats (11 vec4, 176 bytes).
- The root import holds the core only. UI widgets, editor tools, text, layout, glass, glow and WebGPU are separate entry points such as `pixi-silk/ui/button` or `pixi-silk/text`.
- A SmartContainer does nothing until it has a `viewport` (the Application, its renderer, a signal or a rectangle).
- `clear()` keeps the GPU buffers, so clearing and redrawing a SilkGraphics every frame is cheap.
- There are no texture fills and no `svg()` import. Use gradients, and `svgPath(d)` for path data.

## Entry points

Import each from its own path. The root import does not re-export the others.

# Sharp text

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/text/
Area: UI widgets

> SilkText draws labels and readouts from a runtime SDF glyph atlas. Text stays crisp from 0.5x to 100x zoom, with outlines, glows and tabular numbers.

Demo sources run in the docs harness, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `sdf-text` (source):

```ts
import { Container, Graphics, Text } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkText } from 'pixi-silk/text';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Left: PixiJS Text, a bitmap drawn once. Right: SilkText, distance fields drawn per pixel.
// Both zoom around the same point, from 1x to 100x.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [520, 260],
    background: 0x0b0b0d,
    controls: {
        zoom: { type: 'range', label: 'zoom (log)', min: 0, max: 2, step: 0.01, value: 0, unit: '' },
        auto: { type: 'toggle', label: 'animate', value: true },
    },
    setup({ stage, params, tick }) {
        const half = 260;
        const sides: Container[] = [];
        const style = { fontFamily: 'Inter, system-ui, sans-serif', fontSize: 13, fontWeight: '600' as const };

        for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) {
            const side = new Container();
            const mask = new Graphics().rect(i * half, 0, half, 260).fill(0xffffff);
            const make = (text: string, size: number, color: number) =>
                i === 0
                    ? new Text({ text, style: { ...style, fontSize: size, fill: color } })
                    : new SilkText({ text, ...style, fontSize: size, fill: color });
            const title = make('Zoom 42%', 13, 0xffffff);
            const body = make('Labels, readouts and editor text', 9, 0x8e8e93);
            const tiny = make('W 128  H 64', 6, 0x0a84ff);

            title.position.set(70, 112);
            body.position.set(70, 132);
            tiny.position.set(70, 146);
            side.addChild(title, body, tiny);
            side.mask = mask;
            sides.push(side);
            const caption = new Text({
                text: i ? 'SilkText' : 'Text',
                style: { fill: 0x8e8e93, fontSize: 12, fontFamily: 'system-ui' },
            });

            caption.position.set(i * half + 12, 10);
            stage.addChild(side, mask, caption);
        }
        stage.addChild(new Graphics().rect(half - 0.5, 0, 1, 260).fill(0x2c2c2e));
        const readout = new SilkText({
            ...style,
            fontSize: 12,
            fill: 0x8e8e93,
            tabularNumbers: true,
            anchor: { x: 1, y: 0 },
        });

        readout.position.set(508, 10);
        stage.addChild(readout);

        let t = 0;

        tick((_t, dt) => {
            if (params.auto) {
                t += dt;
                // ease in and out between 1x and 100x every 12 s
                params.zoom = 1 - Math.cos((t / 12) * Math.PI * 2);
            }
            const k = 10 ** params.zoom;
            // zoom into the "4" of the title
            const fx = 70 + 44;
            const fy = 112 + 9;

            sides.forEach((side, i) => {
                side.pivot.set(fx, fy);
                side.position.set(i * half + 130, 130);
                side.scale.set(k);
            });
            readout.text = `${k.toFixed(1)}x`;
        });
    },
});
```

### Why text blurs

PixiJS `Text` draws the string once into a canvas and shows it as a texture. Scale it up past its
resolution and you see the pixels. That is fine for a HUD, but not for labels on an infinite
canvas, where the reader zooms in to 300% and beyond.

`SilkText` from `pixi-silk/text` stores each glyph as a **signed distance field** instead: for every
texel, how far it is from the glyph's edge. The shader turns that distance into coverage for
every screen pixel, the same way SilkGraphics draws its shapes. The edge is computed, not
stretched, so it stays sharp at any zoom, rotation and device pixel ratio.

```ts

const label = new SilkText({
    text: 'Frame 1',
    fontFamily: 'Inter, system-ui, sans-serif',
    fontSize: 13,
    fontWeight: 600,
    fill: 0xffffff,
    anchor: 0.5,
});

app.stage.addChild(label);
label.text = 'Frame 2'; // laid out again before the next frame
```

A text is one instanced draw call however long it is. Every text shares one atlas texture.

### Styles

Live demo `sdf-text-styles` (source):

```ts
import { Text } from 'pixi.js';
import { horizontal, SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { SilkText } from 'pixi-silk/text';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Outline, glow, shadow, weight and a gradient all read the same distance field: no filters,
// still one draw call per text. Bottom left: small sizes at 1x, Text above SilkText.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [520, 320],
    background: 0x101014,
    controls: {
        outline: { type: 'range', label: 'outline', min: 0, max: 6, step: 0.5, value: 3, unit: 'px' },
        glow: { type: 'range', label: 'glow', min: 0, max: 12, step: 0.5, value: 8, unit: 'px' },
        dilate: { type: 'range', label: 'weight', min: -1.5, max: 1.5, step: 0.1, value: 0, unit: 'px' },
    },
    setup({ stage, params, tick }) {
        const font = 'Inter, system-ui, sans-serif';
        const card = new SilkGraphics()
            .roundRect(300, 176, 200, 124, 22, 0.6)
            .fill(0x1c1c1e)
            .roundRect(20, 176, 260, 124, 22, 0.6)
            .fill(0x1c1c1e);
        const outlined = new SilkText({
            text: 'OUTLINE',
            fontFamily: font,
            fontSize: 44,
            fontWeight: 900,
            fill: 0xffd60a,
            outline: { width: 3, color: 0x3a1c00 },
            shadow: { color: 0x000000, alpha: 0.6, blur: 6, offsetY: 4 },
        });
        const neon = new SilkText({
            text: 'Neon glow',
            fontFamily: font,
            fontSize: 40,
            fontWeight: 700,
            fill: 0xffe3f1,
            shadow: { color: 0xff2d92, blur: 8 },
        });
        const gradient = new SilkText({
            text: 'Gradient fill',
            fontFamily: font,
            fontSize: 30,
            fontWeight: 800,
            fill: horizontal(['#64d2ff', '#bf5af2', '#ff375f']),
        });
        const readout = new SilkText({
            fontFamily: font,
            fontSize: 40,
            fontWeight: 600,
            fill: 0xffffff,
            tabularNumbers: true,
            anchor: { x: 1, y: 0 },
        });
        const unit = new SilkText({
            text: 'BPM  live',
            fontFamily: font,
            fontSize: 12,
            fontWeight: 600,
            fill: 0xff375f,
        });

        outlined.position.set(20, 20);
        neon.position.set(300, 26);
        gradient.position.set(20, 100);
        readout.position.set(480, 196);
        unit.position.set(320, 270);
        stage.addChild(card, outlined, neon, gradient, readout, unit);

        // small sizes at 1x: PixiJS Text above, SilkText below
        const sample = 'Hamburgefonstiv 0123';

        [11, 12, 13].forEach((size, i) => {
            const y = 190 + i * 34;
            const pixi = new Text({ text: sample, style: { fontFamily: font, fontSize: size, fill: 0xe5e5ea } });
            const silk = new SilkText({ text: sample, fontFamily: font, fontSize: size, fill: 0xe5e5ea });
            const tag = new SilkText({ text: `${size}px`, fontFamily: font, fontSize: 9, fill: 0x8e8e93 });

            pixi.position.set(64, y);
            silk.position.set(64, y + 15);
            tag.position.set(34, y + 9);
            stage.addChild(pixi, silk, tag);
        });

        tick((t) => {
            outlined.outline = params.outline > 0 ? { width: params.outline, color: 0x3a1c00 } : null;
            neon.shadow =
                params.glow > 0 ? { color: 0xff2d92, blur: params.glow * (0.85 + 0.15 * Math.sin(t * 3)) } : null;
            for (const text of [outlined, neon, gradient]) text.dilate = params.dilate;
            readout.text = (72 + 6 * Math.sin(t * 1.3) + 2 * Math.sin(t * 4.1)).toFixed(1);
        });
    },
});
```

Outlines, glows and shadows are cheap with distance fields: they are the same field read at a
different distance. No filters and no extra passes.

```ts

const title = new SilkText({
    text: 'BIG WIN',
    fontSize: 48,
    fontWeight: 900,
    fill: horizontal(['#ffd60a', '#ff9f0a']),
    outline: { width: 3, color: 0x3a1c00 },
    shadow: { color: 0x000000, alpha: 0.6, blur: 6, offsetY: 4 },
});
const readout = new SilkText({ text: '72.4', fontSize: 32, tabularNumbers: true });
```

| Option | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `fontFamily`, `fontSize`, `fontWeight`, `fontStyle` | system UI font, 16, 400, normal | Any CSS font. Size is free, glyphs are baked once |
| `fill` | white | A colour, or a linear, radial or conic gradient over the text box |
| `letterSpacing`, `lineHeight` | 0, font | Spacing in local units. Line height 0 uses the font's own |
| `align`, `wordWrap`, `wordWrapWidth`, `breakWords` | left, off, 100, off | Line layout, like PixiJS `Text` |
| `anchor` | 0 | Point of the text box at the position |
| `outline` | none | `{ width, color, alpha }`, outside the glyph edge |
| `shadow` | none | `{ color, alpha, blur, offsetX, offsetY }`. Without an offset it is a glow |
| `dilate` | 0 | Grows or thins the glyphs, in local units |
| `tabularNumbers` | off | Digits share one width, so changing numbers do not jitter |
| `kerning` | on | Pair kerning measured from Canvas |

Change one field with its setter (`label.fontSize = 18`) or several with `label.set({ ... })`.
Width, height, bounds and hit tests use the text box, as with PixiJS `Text`.

Outlines and glows can reach about a quarter of the font size. Past that, the distance field
runs out and the effect is clamped. For a wider halo, put a blurred SilkGraphics shape behind the text.

### Small sizes

At 11 to 13 px a texel of the atlas is smaller than a screen pixel. SilkText then averages four
samples per pixel instead of one, so thin strokes do not flicker or break up. The bottom left of the
demo above shows PixiJS `Text` and SilkText at 11, 12 and 13 px.

Weight and width match PixiJS `Text`, because the glyphs come from the same Canvas font and the
layout from the same `measureText`. Canvas text can be hinted to the pixel grid on some platforms,
so at 1x its stems can look a touch crisper. From 2x, and whenever the camera zooms, SilkText is the
sharper of the two.

### Widgets

`ValuePill` from `pixi-silk/ui/pill` takes a `label` option: a function that builds its text. Pass
`silkLabel()` for pills that sit under a zooming camera. The widget never imports SilkText on
its own, so apps that do not use it do not ship it.

```ts

const pill = new ValuePill({ label: silkLabel({ tabularNumbers: true }) });

pill.show('42 px');
```

To let people click and type into a text, wrap it in a `TextEditor`. [Editing text](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/editing-text/)
covers the caret, selection, input methods and undo.

### Fonts and the atlas

Glyphs are drawn with Canvas2D at 48 px the first time they appear, then turned into a distance
field. The atlas is one 8-bit texture, 1024 texels wide, that doubles in height up to 4096 as glyphs
arrive. When it is full it starts over, and every text bakes what it needs again.

Load web fonts before the first text uses them, for example with `document.fonts.load('600 16px Inter')`.
Glyphs baked while a font was still loading are baked again once it is ready.

For a different trade-off, make your own atlas and pass it to the texts that should use it:

```ts

const big = new SdfAtlas({ size: 64, radius: 24 }); // sharper corners when zoomed far in, longer glows
const heading = new SilkText({ text: 'Overview', fontSize: 40, atlas: big });
```

### Limits

- **Corners** round off slightly at extreme zoom. A single-channel distance field cannot hold a
  sharp corner. At 48 px the rounding is about a fiftieth of the font size.
- **Emoji and colour fonts** come out as one-colour silhouettes. Use PixiJS `Text` for them.
- **Shaping**: no ligatures, no right-to-left text and no combining marks. Each code point is one glyph.
  Latin, Greek and Cyrillic UI text works. Arabic and Indic scripts need PixiJS `Text`.
- **Kerning** is per pair, measured with `measureText`. It matches Canvas for Latin text.
- **Renderers**: WebGL2 and WebGPU. Pixi's WebGL1 fallback is not supported.

---

# Editing text

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/editing-text/
Area: UI widgets

> TextEditor makes SilkText clickable and editable like a design tool text layer: caret, selection, undo, clipboard and input methods, sharp at any zoom.

Demo sources run in the docs harness, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `text-edit-canvas` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { linear, SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { bindHistoryKeys, History } from 'pixi-silk/history';
import { SilkText, silkLabel } from 'pixi-silk/text';
import { Camera, ZoomControl } from 'pixi-silk/ui/camera';
import { TextEditor } from 'pixi-silk/ui/textedit';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Click a text to edit it, drag to select, double click a word. Pinch or ctrl + scroll to zoom in
// and keep typing: the caret, the selection and the glyphs stay sharp. Scroll pans.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [760, 440],
    background: 0x151517,
    touchAction: 'none',
    ownsCamera: true,
    setup({ silk }) {
        const app = silk.app;
        const world = new Container();
        const camera = new Camera({ world, element: app.canvas });
        const zoom = new ZoomControl(camera, { label: silkLabel() });
        // one undo stack for the whole canvas: text edits sit in it with everything else
        const history = new History();
        const font = 'Inter, system-ui, sans-serif';

        app.stage.addChild(world, zoom);

        const card = new SilkGraphics()
            .roundRect(0, 0, 420, 260, 28, 0.6)
            .fill(linear([0x2c2c30, 0x1c1c1f], { from: [0, 0], to: [0, 1] }))
            .roundRect(0.5, 0.5, 419, 259, 28, 0.6)
            .stroke({ color: 0xffffff, alpha: 0.08, width: 1 });
        const caption = new SilkText({ text: 'Frame 1', fontFamily: font, fontSize: 11, fill: 0x8e8e93 });

        caption.position.set(2, -18);
        world.addChild(card, caption);

        const title = new TextEditor({
            text: 'Edit me',
            fontFamily: font,
            fontSize: 30,
            fontWeight: 700,
            fill: 0xffffff,
            multiline: false,
            history,
            element: app.canvas,
        });
        const body = new TextEditor({
            text: 'Text on an infinite canvas. Zoom to 800% and the caret is still one crisp pixel and a half wide.\n\nDouble click a word, triple click a paragraph.',
            fontFamily: font,
            fontSize: 15,
            lineHeight: 22,
            fill: 0xc7c7cc,
            width: 356,
            placeholder: 'Write something',
            history,
            element: app.canvas,
        });

        title.position.set(32, 30);
        body.position.set(32, 86);
        world.addChild(title, body);

        // a tiny caption follows the title as you type
        const count = new SilkText({ fontFamily: font, fontSize: 11, fill: 0x636366, tabularNumbers: true });

        count.position.set(32, 230);
        world.addChild(count);
        const update = () => {
            const words = body.value.split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean).length;

            count.text = `${title.value.length} characters in the title, ${words} words below`;
        };
        const stops = [title.state.text.subscribe(update), body.state.text.subscribe(update), bindHistoryKeys(history)];

        update();
        const place = () =>
            zoom.position.set(app.screen.width - zoom.size[0] - 12, app.screen.height - zoom.size[1] - 12);

        app.renderer.on('resize', place);
        place();
        camera.fit({ x: -20, y: -40, width: 460, height: 320 }, 24, false);

        return () => {
            for (const stop of stops) stop();
            app.renderer.off('resize', place);
            title.destroy();
            body.destroy();
            camera.destroy();
        };
    },
});
```

### A text layer you can type into

`TextEditor` from `pixi-silk/ui/textedit` wraps a [SilkText](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/text/) with a caret, a selection
and keyboard input. It lives in the scene like any display object, so it pans and zooms with a
[camera](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/canvas-and-measurements/) and stays sharp at 800%.

```ts

declare const world: Container;
declare const canvas: HTMLCanvasElement;

const note = new TextEditor({
    text: 'Edit me',
    fontSize: 15,
    width: 320, // wraps at 320, grows in height
    placeholder: 'Write something',
    element: canvas,
});

note.on('change', (text: string) => console.log('saved', text));
world.addChild(note);
```

Click to place the caret, drag to select and Shift + click to extend. A double click selects a word
and a triple click its paragraph. Words come from `Intl.Segmenter`, so they follow the language.

### How typing works

The keys do not go to the canvas. On focus, the editor puts a hidden `<textarea>` on the page, right
at the caret, and keeps its text and selection in step with its own. The browser types into it, and
the editor applies each change.

That is why the platform's own text input works:

- **Input methods** for Japanese, Chinese and Korean, and dead keys for accents. The text being
  composed shows inline with an underline, and becomes one undo step when you confirm it.
- Mobile keyboards, autocorrect and the emoji picker.
- Copy, cut and paste, with the system clipboard.

The field follows the caret as you type, pan or zoom, so input method popups open next to the text.
Pass `element` (usually `app.canvas`) to place it. Without it, the editor uses the element of the first press.

### Keys

| Keys | What they do |
| --- | --- |
| Left, Right | Move by grapheme, so an emoji or an accented letter is one step |
| Option + Left / Right on Mac, Ctrl elsewhere | Move by word |
| Up, Down | Move by line, keeping the column you started from |
| Home, End, or cmd + Left / Right on Mac | Start and end of the line on screen |
| Shift with any of these | Extend the selection |
| cmd or ctrl + A | Select all |
| Backspace, Delete | Delete a grapheme, or a word with Option or Ctrl |
| Enter | A new line, or commit in a single-line field |
| cmd or ctrl + Enter | Commit and leave a multi-line field |
| Tab | Move to the next field, or insert a tab with `tab: 'insert'` |
| Escape | Leave the field |
| cmd or ctrl + Z, Shift + cmd or ctrl + Z | Undo and redo |

### Fields and forms

Live demo `text-edit-form` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { watch } from 'pixi-silk/signal';
import { SilkText } from 'pixi-silk/text';
import { TextEditor } from 'pixi-silk/ui/textedit';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Single-line fields drawn on the canvas. Tab moves to the next field, Enter in the last one sends
// the form. Japanese or Chinese input, dead keys and the emoji picker type into them as usual.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [520, 330],
    background: 0x0f0f11,
    setup({ silk, stage }) {
        const font = 'Inter, system-ui, sans-serif';
        const fields: TextEditor[] = [];
        const stops: (() => void)[] = [];
        const field = (label: string, y: number, options: ConstructorParameters<typeof TextEditor>[0]) => {
            const row = new Container();
            const name = new SilkText({ text: label, fontFamily: font, fontSize: 12, fontWeight: 600, fill: 0x8e8e93 });
            const box = new SilkGraphics();
            const editor = new TextEditor({
                fontFamily: font,
                fontSize: 15,
                fill: 0xf2f2f7,
                multiline: false,
                width: 276,
                element: silk.app.canvas,
                ...options,
            });

            editor.position.set(12, 31);
            // a press anywhere on the box focuses the field
            box.eventMode = 'static';
            box.cursor = 'text';
            box.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
                (e.nativeEvent as Event).preventDefault();
                editor.focus();
            });
            row.addChild(name, box, editor);
            row.position.set(40, y);
            // the box lights up while its field has focus
            stops.push(
                watch(editor.state.focused, (on) => {
                    box.clear()
                        .roundRect(0, 20, 300, 40, 10, 0.6)
                        .fill(on ? 0x1c1c22 : 0x18181b)
                        .roundRect(0, 20, 300, 40, 10, 0.6)
                        .stroke({ color: on ? 0x0a84ff : 0x3a3a3c, width: on ? 1.5 : 1, alignment: 'inside' });
                }),
            );
            stage.addChild(row);
            fields.push(editor);

            return editor;
        };

        const name = field('Name', 24, { placeholder: 'Ada Lovelace' });
        const email = field('Email', 104, { placeholder: 'ada@example.com' });
        const code = field('Promo code, 8 characters at most', 184, { placeholder: 'SILK2026', maxLength: 8 });
        const result = new SilkText({
            fontFamily: font,
            fontSize: 12,
            fill: 0x30d158,
            wordWrap: true,
            wordWrapWidth: 150,
        });
        const button = new Container();
        const pill = new SilkGraphics().pill(0, 0, 140, 40).fill(0x0a84ff);
        const label = new SilkText({ text: 'Send', fontFamily: font, fontSize: 14, fontWeight: 600, anchor: 0.5 });

        label.position.set(70, 20);
        button.addChild(pill, label);
        button.position.set(360, 228);
        button.eventMode = 'static';
        button.cursor = 'pointer';
        result.position.set(360, 52);
        stage.addChild(button, result);
        const send = () => {
            const values = [name.value || 'no name', email.value || 'no email', code.value || 'no code'];

            result.text = `Sent\n${values.join('\n')}`;
            TextEditor.active?.blur();
        };

        button.on('pointertap', send);
        code.on('submit', send);
        // Enter in the first fields moves on, like a sign-up form
        name.on('submit', () => email.focus(true));
        email.on('submit', () => code.focus(true));
        stops.push(TextEditor.chain(fields));

        return () => {
            for (const stop of stops) stop();
            for (const f of fields) f.destroy();
        };
    },
});
```

With `multiline: false` the editor is an input. Enter commits instead of breaking the line. A
`width` clips the text and scrolls it to keep the caret in view. `TextEditor.chain()` links fields in
Tab order.

```ts

const email = new TextEditor({ multiline: false, width: 260, placeholder: 'ada@example.com' });
const code = new TextEditor({ multiline: false, width: 260, maxLength: 8 });

TextEditor.chain([email, code]);
code.on('submit', () => console.log(email.value, code.value));
```

| Option | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `multiline` | true | A text area, or a single-line input |
| `width` | none | Wraps a text area, clips and scrolls an input |
| `placeholder` | empty | Shown while the text is empty |
| `maxLength` | none | Longest text in UTF-16 code units, like `maxlength`. Never splits an emoji |
| `readOnly` | false | Selectable and copyable, not editable |
| `selectable` | true | False turns pointer input off |
| `enter` | `newline` or `commit` | `newline`, `commit` or `blur` |
| `tab` | `next` | `next` emits `tab` and leaves, `insert` types a tab |
| `history` | its own | A `HistoryLike` to share the app's undo stack |
| `theme` | blue | Caret, selection and underline colours |

Every SilkText style option works too: font, size, weight, fill, letter spacing, line height and alignment.

### Read-only text

Live demo `text-edit-readonly` (source):

```ts
import { SilkText } from 'pixi-silk/text';
import { TextEditor } from 'pixi-silk/ui/textedit';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// A read-only paragraph: select with the mouse or a long press, cmd or ctrl + C copies.
// There is no caret and typing does nothing.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [520, 260],
    background: 0x0b0b0d,
    setup({ silk, stage }) {
        const font = 'Inter, system-ui, sans-serif';
        const paragraph = new TextEditor({
            text: 'Release notes. Text layers now edit on the canvas. Selection, the clipboard and input methods go through a hidden text field, so they behave the way the platform does. Double click a word to select it.',
            fontFamily: font,
            fontSize: 15,
            lineHeight: 23,
            fill: 0xd1d1d6,
            width: 440,
            readOnly: true,
            element: silk.app.canvas,
            theme: { selection: 0xffd60a, selectionAlpha: 0.28 },
        });
        const readout = new SilkText({ fontFamily: font, fontSize: 12, fill: 0x8e8e93 });

        paragraph.position.set(40, 36);
        readout.position.set(40, 214);
        stage.addChild(paragraph, readout);
        const stop = paragraph.state.selection.subscribe(() => {
            const n = paragraph.selectedText.length;

            readout.text = n ? `${n} characters selected. Cmd or ctrl + C copies them.` : '';
        });

        return () => {
            stop();
            paragraph.destroy();
        };
    },
});
```

`readOnly: true` keeps clicking, selecting and copying, and drops the caret and editing. Use it for
release notes, logs or error messages that people want to copy.

### Events, signals and undo

The editor emits `focus`, `blur`, `input` on every edit, `change` when the text was committed with a
new value, `submit` on Enter in a single-line field, and `tab`. The same state is on
`editor.state` as [signals](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/selection-and-history/): `text`, `committed`, `focused`, `selection`
and `composing`.

Undo is built in. Consecutive typing folds into one step, and a click or an arrow key starts the
next. To put text edits in line with the rest of an editor, pass its history:

```ts

const history = new History();
const title = new TextEditor({ text: 'Frame 1', multiline: false, history });

bindHistoryKeys(history); // cmd + Z outside the field undoes text edits too
```

### Hit testing for other tools

The caret math is in the layout, so other tools can use it. `SilkText` has `caretAt(point)` for the
caret nearest to a local point. `caretRect(index)` gives where a caret sits and `rangeRects(from, to)`
the highlight boxes of a range. They follow wrapped lines, alignment, letter spacing, line height,
empty lines and trailing spaces. `pixi-silk/text` also exports the same functions on a `TextLayout`,
plus `graphemeStops`, `wordAt` and the word jumps.

```ts

const label = new SilkText({ text: 'Hello world', wordWrap: true, wordWrapWidth: 60 });
const hit = label.caretAt({ x: 30, y: 8 });
const caret = label.caretRect(hit.index, hit.upstream);

console.log(hit.index, caret.x, label.rangeRects(0, 5));
```

### Limits

- **Right-to-left and mixed direction text** is not supported. Arabic and Hebrew lay out left to
  right, and the caret moves in source order.
- **Emoji** count as one caret step, but draw as one-colour silhouettes, as in [SilkText](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/text/).
- **Shaping**: no ligatures and no combining marks drawn on their base letter. Each code point is a glyph.
- **Spell check** underlines are off: the hidden field is invisible.

---

# Radial menus and buttons

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/radial-menus-and-buttons/
Area: UI widgets

> Marking menus with nested rings, falloff and per-device tuning, plus buttons that animate in and out and ignore input while they move. Tree-shakeable.

Demo sources run in the docs harness, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `radial-menu` (source):

```ts
import { Container, Text } from 'pixi.js';
import type { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { SilkButton } from 'pixi-silk/ui/button';
import { bindRadialTrigger, type RadialItem, RadialMenu } from 'pixi-silk/ui/radial';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Vector icons: drawn once in white around (0, 0); the menu tints them.
type Icon = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => void;
const pointer: Icon = (g, s) => {
    g.triangle(-s * 0.3, -s * 0.42, s * 0.34, s * 0.06, -s * 0.06, s * 0.16, 2).fill(0xffffff);
    g.line(-s * 0.02, s * 0.12, s * 0.16, s * 0.42).stroke({ width: s * 0.12, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
};
const move: Icon = (g, s) => {
    const r = s * 0.42;

    g.line(-r, 0, r, 0).stroke({ width: s * 0.09, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
    g.line(0, -r, 0, r).stroke({ width: s * 0.09, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
    for (let i = 0; i < 4; i++) {
        const a = (i * Math.PI) / 2;

        g.regularPoly(Math.cos(a) * r, Math.sin(a) * r, s * 0.14, 3, a + Math.PI / 2, 1).fill(0xffffff);
    }
};
const rotate: Icon = (g, s) => {
    g.arcSweep(0, 0, s * 0.32, -Math.PI * 0.35, Math.PI * 1.6).stroke({
        width: s * 0.1,
        cap: 'round',
        color: 0xffffff,
    });
    g.regularPoly(s * 0.18, -s * 0.3, s * 0.14, 3, Math.PI / 2, 1).fill(0xffffff);
};
const scale: Icon = (g, s) => {
    g.roundRect(-s * 0.4, -s * 0.1, s * 0.5, s * 0.5, 3).stroke({ width: s * 0.08, color: 0xffffff });
    g.roundRect(-s * 0.1, -s * 0.42, s * 0.52, s * 0.52, 3).fill(0xffffff);
};
const gear: Icon = (g, s) => {
    g.circle(0, 0, s * 0.33).stroke({ width: s * 0.16, dash: [s * 0.13, s * 0.13], color: 0xffffff });
    g.circle(0, 0, s * 0.22).stroke({ width: s * 0.1, color: 0xffffff });
};
const more: Icon = (g, s) => {
    for (const x of [-1, 0, 1]) g.circle(x * s * 0.28, 0, s * 0.09).fill(0xffffff);
};
const heart: Icon = (g, s) => g.heart(0, 0, s * 0.8, 0.1).fill(0xffffff);
const star: Icon = (g, s) => g.star(0, 0, 5, s * 0.45, undefined, 0, 1.5).fill(0xffffff);
const plus: Icon = (g, s) => {
    g.line(-s * 0.35, 0, s * 0.35, 0).stroke({ width: s * 0.13, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
    g.line(0, -s * 0.35, 0, s * 0.35).stroke({ width: s * 0.13, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
};

const tools: RadialItem[] = [
    { id: 'select', label: 'SELECT', icon: pointer },
    {
        id: 'move',
        label: 'MOVE',
        icon: move,
        children: [
            { id: 'intuitive', label: 'Intuitive' },
            { id: 'axis', label: 'Axis' },
            { id: 'free', label: 'Free' },
        ],
    },
    {
        id: 'rotate',
        label: 'ROTATE',
        icon: rotate,
        children: [
            { id: 'uniform', label: 'Uniform' },
            { id: 'axis', label: 'Axis' },
            { id: 'free', label: 'Free' },
        ],
    },
    { id: 'scale', label: 'SCALE', icon: scale },
    { id: 'settings', label: 'SETTINGS', icon: gear, disabled: true },
    { id: 'more', label: 'MORE', icon: more },
];

// Press anywhere (or the round button) and drag to an item, or tap to keep the menu open.
// The `+` button opens a half ring that turns away from the edge it sits on.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [640, 400],
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        closeOnLeave: { type: 'toggle', label: 'close on leave', value: false },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const surface = new Container();
        const status = new Text({
            text: 'press, drag, release, or tap',
            style: { fill: 0x8e8e93, fontFamily: 'system-ui, sans-serif', fontSize: 13 },
        });
        const bounds = { x: 0, y: 0, width: 640, height: 400 };
        const report = (_item: RadialItem, path: readonly RadialItem[]) => {
            status.text = `picked  ${path.map((i) => i.id).join(' › ')}`;
        };
        const menu = new RadialMenu({ items: tools, bounds, ticker, onSelect: report });
        const quick = new RadialMenu({
            items: [
                { id: 'heart', label: 'LIKE', icon: heart },
                { id: 'star', label: 'FAVOURITE', icon: star },
                { id: 'plus', label: 'ADD', icon: plus },
                { id: 'more', label: 'MORE', icon: more },
            ],
            sweep: Math.PI * 0.9,
            innerRadius: 34,
            outerRadius: 92,
            bounds,
            ticker,
            onSelect: report,
        });
        const hub = new SilkButton({ shape: 'circle', height: 64, icon: gear, iconSize: 28, ticker });
        const add = new SilkButton({ shape: 'circle', height: 52, icon: plus, iconSize: 22, ticker });

        surface.eventMode = 'static';
        surface.hitArea = { contains: (x, y) => x >= 0 && y >= 0 && x <= 640 && y <= 400 };
        status.position.set(20, 18);
        hub.position.set(200, 210);
        add.position.set(586, 346);
        stage.addChild(surface, status, hub, add, menu, quick);

        bindRadialTrigger(surface, menu, { at: 'pointer' });
        bindRadialTrigger(hub, menu);
        bindRadialTrigger(add, quick);
        menu.state.hover.subscribe((item) => {
            if (item) status.text = `${menu.state.kind.value}: ${item.id}${item.disabled ? ' (disabled)' : ''}`;
        });

        tick(() => {
            menu.closeOnLeave = quick.closeOnLeave = params.closeOnLeave;
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

### Import only what you use

Each widget has its own entry point. Nothing else comes along: the menu does not pull in the button,
and `pixi-silk/signal` has no Pixi code at all.

```ts
```

### Radial menus

```ts

const menu = new RadialMenu({
    items: [
        { id: 'select', label: 'SELECT', icon: drawPointer },
        { id: 'move', label: 'MOVE', icon: drawMove, children: [{ id: 'axis', label: 'Axis' }, { id: 'free', label: 'Free' }] },
        { id: 'delete', label: 'DELETE', icon: drawTrash, disabled: true },
    ],
    sweep: Math.PI * 2, // Math.PI for a half ring
    bounds: { x: 0, y: 0, width: app.screen.width, height: app.screen.height },
    onSelect: (item, path) => console.log(path.map((i) => i.id).join(' > ')),
});

app.stage.addChild(menu);
bindRadialTrigger(toolButton, menu); // or menu.openAt(x, y, event)
```

How it behaves:

- **Marking.** Press, drag towards an item, release: picked in one gesture. A tap opens the menu
  and leaves it up for a second tap.
- **Angle, not hit boxes.** Past the dead zone the whole wedge counts, out to the falloff
  distance. A fast flick lands on its item even when the pointer overshoots the ring.
- **Hysteresis.** The current item holds until the pointer is clearly past its edge, so a shaky
  finger on a boundary does not flicker between two items.
- **It stays.** Moving away does not close the menu. Set `closeOnLeave` to close it after the
  pointer stays outside the falloff area for a grace delay.
- **Nested rings.** Items with `children` open the next ring around themselves, to any depth.
- **Edges.** Full rings slide inside `bounds`. Partial rings turn to face the inside instead.
- **Per device.** Dead zone, falloff, size, tap time and hysteresis come from an input profile per
  pointer kind. Touch gets bigger targets and more forgiveness. Override any of it with `profiles`.

Icons are a string, a texture, a container or a function that draws with `SilkGraphics` in white.
The menu tints them, so hover colours cost no redraw.

### Buttons that know when to stay still

Live demo `spin-dock` (source):

```ts
import { Text } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { signal } from 'pixi-silk/signal';
import { SilkButton } from 'pixi-silk/ui/button';
import { ButtonDock } from 'pixi-silk/ui/dock';
import { SegmentedSwitch } from 'pixi-silk/ui/modes';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

const play = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => g.regularPoly(s * 0.06, 0, s * 0.42, 3, Math.PI / 2, 2).fill(0xffffff);
const minus = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) =>
    g.line(-s * 0.3, 0, s * 0.3, 0).stroke({ width: s * 0.14, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
const plus = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    minus(g, s);
    g.line(0, -s * 0.3, 0, s * 0.3).stroke({ width: s * 0.14, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
};
// a lightning bolt as two rounded triangles (closed polygons cannot be filled yet)
const bolt = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    g.triangle(s * 0.12, -s * 0.5, -s * 0.3, s * 0.08, s * 0.08, s * 0.02, 1).fill(0xffffff);
    g.triangle(-s * 0.12, s * 0.5, s * 0.3, -s * 0.08, -s * 0.08, -s * 0.02, 1).fill(0xffffff);
};

// Tap SPIN for one spin, hold it for five. While the reels run, the side buttons leave, the speed
// switch locks and the spin button turns busy: nothing can be pressed twice. The bet lives in a signal.
const SPEED: Record<string, number> = { normal: 1.1, turbo: 0.6, quick: 0.3 };
export default defineDemo({
    size: [560, 340],
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        enabled: { type: 'toggle', label: 'spin enabled', value: true },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const bet = signal(1);
        const reels = new SilkGraphics();
        const label = new Text({
            text: '',
            style: { fill: 0xf2f2f7, fontFamily: 'system-ui, sans-serif', fontSize: 15, fontWeight: '600' },
        });
        const side = { ticker, theme: { fill: 0x2c2c2e, fillHover: 0x3a3a3c, fillPress: 0x1c1c1e } };
        const less = new SilkButton({
            ...side,
            shape: 'circle',
            height: 48,
            icon: minus,
            onPress: () => bet.update((b) => Math.max(1, b - 1)),
        });
        const more = new SilkButton({
            ...side,
            shape: 'circle',
            height: 48,
            icon: plus,
            onPress: () => bet.update((b) => Math.min(10, b + 1)),
        });
        const speed = new SegmentedSwitch({
            ticker,
            value: 'normal',
            segmentWidth: 78,
            height: 34,
            options: [
                { id: 'normal', label: 'Normal' },
                { id: 'turbo', icon: bolt },
                { id: 'quick', label: 'Quick' },
            ],
        });
        const spin = new SilkButton({
            ticker,
            shape: 'circle',
            height: 76,
            icon: play,
            iconSize: 30,
            holdMs: 450,
            theme: { fill: 0x30d158, fillHover: 0x4ade80, fillPress: 0x248a3d },
            onPress: () => run(1),
            onHold: () => run(5),
        });
        const dock = new ButtonDock({ ticker, gap: 14 }).add(less, spin, more);
        let spinning = 0;
        let duration = 1.1;
        let left = 0;

        bet.subscribe((b) => (label.text = `bet ${b}`));
        label.text = `bet ${bet.value}`;
        label.anchor.set(0.5);
        label.position.set(90, 34);
        speed.position.set(400, 34);
        dock.position.set(280, 282);
        stage.addChild(reels, label, speed, dock);

        async function run(count: number) {
            left = count;
            spin.state.busy.set(true);
            speed.state.enabled.set(false);
            await Promise.all([less.hide(), more.hide()]);
            while (left-- > 0) {
                duration = SPEED[speed.state.value.value] ?? 1.1;
                spinning = duration;
                await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, duration * 1000));
            }
            spin.state.busy.set(false);
            speed.state.enabled.set(true);
            await dock.showAll();
        }

        tick((t, dt) => {
            spin.state.enabled.set(params.enabled);
            spinning = Math.max(0, spinning - dt);
            reels.clear();
            for (let c = 0; c < 5; c++) {
                const x = 60 + c * 92;
                const run = spinning > 0 && spinning < duration * (1 - c * 0.05) ? 1 : 0;

                reels.roundRect(x, 62, 80, 168, 14, 0.6).fill(0x1c1c1e);
                for (let r = 0; r < 3; r++) {
                    const y = 70 + r * 54 + (run ? ((t * 900 + c * 40) % 54) - 27 : 0);
                    const hue = [0xff453a, 0xffd60a, 0x0a84ff, 0xbf5af2][(r + c + (run ? Math.floor(t * 20) : 0)) % 4];

                    reels.roundRect(x + 12, y + 6, 56, 40, 10, 0.6).fill({ color: hue, alpha: run ? 0.55 : 0.9 });
                }
            }
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

```ts

const spin = new SilkButton({ shape: 'circle', height: 76, icon: drawPlay, holdMs: 450, onPress: spinOnce, onHold: autoplay });
const turbo = new SilkButton({ label: 'Turbo' });

await turbo.hide();        // resolves when the animation ends
spin.state.busy.set(true); // spinner, input off
spin.state.active.subscribe((on) => console.log('accepts input', on));
```

A button only accepts input when it is shown, settled, enabled and not busy. `show()` and `hide()`
return promises that resolve `false` if the animation was reversed half way. A press that slides a
little off the button still counts on release.

`ButtonDock` lines buttons up and closes the gap when one hides. Slot sizes follow each button's
animated appearance, so the reflow and the fade move together.

### State in signals

Every widget keeps its state in `state`: small signals you can read, write and subscribe to. A
signal is a value with `subscribe`. There is no tracking magic, so it plugs into React, MobX or
plain callbacks.

```ts

const bet = signal(1);
const label = computed([bet], () => `bet ${bet.value}`);

bet.subscribe((now, before) => console.log(before, '->', now));
batch(() => {
    bet.set(2);
    bet.set(3);
}); // one notification
```

Widgets animate on `Ticker.shared` and leave the ticker once nothing moves. Pass `ticker` to use
your application's ticker, or `null` to step them yourself with `tick(dt)`.

---

# Layout and smart containers

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/layout/
Area: UI widgets

> Place HUD boxes on any screen the way slot games do, with portrait and landscape records, then lay out what is inside them with a small flex engine.

Demo sources run in the docs harness, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `layout-hud` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { SilkText } from 'pixi-silk/text';
import {
    DEVICES,
    FlexContainer,
    layoutDebug,
    SmartContainer,
    type SmartContainerOptions,
    simulateViewport,
} from 'pixi-silk/ui/layout';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// A slot game HUD built from SmartContainers, each placed by a portrait and a landscape record.
// The balance pins to the top left corner, the menu to the top right, the reels stay centred and
// fit, and the bar stretches along the bottom with a flex row inside. Pick a device: the page
// previews it letterboxed, and the HUD glides to the new breakpoint. Press L, or tick debug, for
// the layout overlay. Hover a box for its readout.

const GOLD = 0xffd60a;
const font = 'system-ui, -apple-system, Segoe UI, Roboto, sans-serif';

/** A 5 by 3 reel window with a few vector symbols, 1040x640 design units. */
function reels(): Container {
    const g = new SilkGraphics();

    g.roundRect(0, 0, 1040, 640, 40, 0.6).fill(0x1c1c1e);
    for (let c = 0; c < 5; c++) {
        for (let r = 0; r < 3; r++) {
            const x = 20 + c * 200 + 100;
            const y = 20 + r * 200 + 100;
            const kind = (c * 7 + r * 3) % 5;

            g.roundRect(x - 88, y - 88, 176, 176, 32, 0.6).fill(0x2c2c2e);
            if (kind === 0) g.heart(x, y + 4, 104, 8).fill(0xff375f);
            else if (kind === 1) g.circle(x, y, 52).fill(0x0a84ff);
            else if (kind === 2) g.regularPoly(x, y, 60, 6, 0, 12).fill(0x30d158);
            else if (kind === 3) g.triangle(x, y - 56, x + 56, y + 44, x - 56, y + 44, 12).fill(0xbf5af2);
            else g.regularPoly(x, y, 60, 4, Math.PI / 4, 8).fill(GOLD);
        }
    }

    return g;
}

/** A pill with a label and a value. The value shrinks to fit the pill, never below 0.55. */
function pill(label: string, value: string, color: number): { box: FlexContainer; value: SilkText } {
    const box = new FlexContainer({ layout: { direction: 'column', padding: [14, 28], gap: 2 } });
    const bg = new SilkGraphics();
    const v = new SilkText({ text: value, fontFamily: font, fontWeight: 700, fontSize: 52, fill: color });

    box.add(bg, {
        position: 'absolute',
        inset: 0,
        onSize: (w, h) => bg.clear().roundRect(0, 0, w, h, 30, 0.6).fill(0x1c1c1e),
    });
    box.add(new SilkText({ text: label, fontFamily: font, fontSize: 24, fill: 0x8e8e93 }));
    box.add(v, { fit: 'scale-down', minScale: 0.55 });

    return { box, value: v };
}

/** A round button with a label on it. */
function button(radius: number, color: number, text: string): Container {
    const c = new Container();

    c.addChild(
        new SilkGraphics().circle(0, 0, radius).fill(color),
        new SilkText({ text, fontFamily: font, fontWeight: 800, fontSize: radius * 0.4, fill: 0xffffff, anchor: 0.5 }),
    );
    c.eventMode = 'static';
    c.cursor = 'pointer';

    return c;
}

const money = (n: number) => `$${n.toLocaleString('en-US', { minimumFractionDigits: 2, maximumFractionDigits: 2 })}`;

export default defineDemo({
    size: [920, 520],
    background: 0x0b0b0d,
    controls: {
        device: { type: 'select', label: 'device', options: Object.keys(DEVICES), value: 'desktop' },
        notch: { type: 'toggle', label: 'notch', value: false },
        debug: { type: 'toggle', label: 'debug', value: false },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        // a device window previewed inside the page. Every SmartContainer follows it.
        const sim = simulateViewport({ area: { x: 0, y: 0, width: 920, height: 520 }, device: 'desktop' });
        const debug = layoutDebug(silk.app, { key: 'KeyL' });
        const all: SmartContainer[] = [];
        const make = (label: string, options: SmartContainerOptions) => {
            const c = new SmartContainer({
                ticker: silk.app.ticker,
                viewport: sim,
                // games usually snap. The demo glides on the switch to show where things go.
                switchTransition: { stiffness: 160, damping: 22 },
                ...options,
            });

            c.label = label;
            all.push(c);
            sim.content.addChild(c);

            return c;
        };

        // background: covers the whole window, under the notch too
        const bg = make('background', {
            portraitData: { width: 1080, height: 1920, fitCover: true, ignoreSafeArea: true },
            landscapeData: { width: 1920, height: 1080, fitCover: true, ignoreSafeArea: true },
        });

        bg.addChild(new SilkGraphics().rect(0, 0, 1920, 1920).fill(0x101014));

        // reels: centred, fit to the reference resolution, moved up to leave room for the bar
        make('reels', {
            portraitData: { width: 1040, height: 640, correctionOffsetY: -200 },
            landscapeData: { width: 1040, height: 640, correctionOffsetY: -50 },
        }).addChild(reels());

        // balance pinned to the top left corner, menu to the top right
        const corner = make('balance', {
            layout: { padding: 24 },
            portraitData: { align: 'left', valign: 'top', width: 440, height: 170 },
            landscapeData: { align: 'left', valign: 'top', width: 440, height: 170 },
        });

        corner.add(pill('BALANCE', '$12,480.00', 0xffffff).box, { grow: 1 });

        make('menu', {
            layout: { justifyContent: 'center', alignItems: 'center' },
            portraitData: { align: 'right', valign: 'top', width: 140, height: 140 },
            landscapeData: { align: 'right', valign: 'top', width: 140, height: 140 },
        }).add(button(40, 0x2c2c2e, 'MENU'));

        // the bar: along the bottom and as wide as the window, with a flex row inside
        const bar = make('bar', {
            layout: {
                padding: [16, 32, 28],
                gap: 24,
                alignItems: 'center',
                justifyContent: 'space-between',
                media: [{ breakpoint: 'portrait', style: { wrap: 'wrap', justifyContent: 'center', rowGap: 16 } }],
            },
            portraitData: { valign: 'bottom', width: 1080, height: 420, extendWidth: true },
            landscapeData: { valign: 'bottom', width: 1920, height: 200, extendWidth: true },
        });
        const win = pill('WIN', '$0.00', GOLD);
        const bet = pill('BET', '$2.00', 0xffffff);
        const spin = button(76, 0x30d158, 'SPIN');

        // in portrait the spin button takes a row of its own above the two pills
        bar.add(spin, { media: [{ breakpoint: 'portrait', style: { basis: '100%' } }] });
        bar.add(bet.box, { grow: 1, basis: 0, maxWidth: 440 });
        bar.add(win.box, { grow: 1, basis: 0, maxWidth: 440 });

        spin.on('pointertap', () => {
            win.value.text = money(Math.random() < 0.3 ? 0 : 10 ** (1 + Math.random() * 5));
            // new text, new size: measure it again
            win.box.invalidate(win.value);
        });

        stage.addChild(sim);

        let last = '';
        let debugParam = false;

        tick(() => {
            // the checkbox and the L key both toggle the overlay
            if (params.debug !== debugParam) {
                debugParam = params.debug;
                if (debug.enabled !== debugParam) debug.toggle();
            }
            const key = `${params.device}|${params.notch}`;

            if (key === last) return;
            last = key;
            const [w, h] = DEVICES[params.device];

            for (const c of all)
                c.setSafeArea(params.notch ? (w > h ? { left: 44, right: 44 } : { top: 47, bottom: 34 }) : {});
            sim.setDevice(params.device);
        });

        return () => {
            debug.destroy();
            for (const c of all) c.destroy({ children: true });
        };
    },
});
```

The HUD above is five SmartContainers. Each one places a design box in the window from a
portrait record and a landscape record:

- **Balance** pins to the top left corner, and the **menu** to the top right.
- **Reels** stay centred and fit the reference resolution.
- **The bar** runs along the bottom, as wide as the window, with a flex row inside.
- **The background** covers the whole window, under the notch too.

Everything lives in one entry point, plus the headless engine:

```ts
```

### Which one

- **SmartContainer** places a whole block on the screen: a HUD corner, the reels, a bottom bar, a
  background. It anchors and scales to the window from layout records, per breakpoint.
- **FlexContainer** arranges children inside a block: a row of buttons, a pill with a label and a
  value, a card list. It knows nothing about the window.

They compose. A SmartContainer with a `layout` is a flex container for its children, and it can
hold FlexContainers. A FlexContainer can hold a SmartContainer, which then places itself and
takes no slot. Both run on one update loop: layout once per frame after a change, springs while
something moves, nothing when idle.

### SmartContainer

```ts

const reels = new SmartContainer({
    viewport: app,
    portraitData: { width: 1040, height: 640, correctionOffsetY: -200 },
    landscapeData: { width: 1040, height: 640, correctionOffsetY: -50 },
});

app.stage.addChild(reels);
```

A SmartContainer has two layout records. It uses `portraitData` when the window is taller than
wide and `landscapeData` otherwise. Each record describes a design box and how it sits in the
window:

- **`width` and `height`** are the design box, in design units.
- **`viewportWidth` and `viewportHeight`** are the reference resolution. The defaults are 1080x1920
  in portrait and 1920x1080 in landscape.
- **`align` and `valign`** anchor the scaled box: `left`, `center` or `right`, and `top`, `center` or `bottom`.
- **`fitCover`** scales to cover the window instead of fitting inside it.
- **`stretchWidth` and `stretchHeight`** scale one axis so the box spans the window. That distorts
  the content, which suits backgrounds.
- **`correctionScaleX` and `correctionScaleY`** multiply the scale. `correctionOffsetX` and
  `correctionOffsetY` move the box in design units.

The scale is the window size over the reference resolution, the smaller ratio or the larger one
with `fitCover`. Stretch replaces it per axis, and the corrections apply last. The position is the
window origin, plus the aligned offset of the scaled box, plus the correction offset times the scale.

These names and numbers match the SmartContainer many slot games already use, so a game can bring
its records over unchanged. `placeBox(record, window)` gives the same placement without a container.

#### What it adds

- **A viewport source, not window listeners.** Pass an Application or a renderer, and the
  container updates on its `resize`. A signal of a rectangle works too. With a plain rectangle,
  call `updateContainer()` after you change it.
- **Named breakpoints.** `breakpoints` lists records with a `when(width, height)` test. The first
  match wins, before portrait and landscape.
- **Safe areas.** `safeArea` insets, in screen pixels, shrink the window the boxes are placed in.
  A record with `ignoreSafeArea` places its box in the whole window, for backgrounds.
- **A glide on switch.** With `switchTransition`, the container glides on a spring to its new
  place and scale when the breakpoint changes. Resizes within a breakpoint snap. It is off by
  default, as games usually want.
- **`extendWidth` and `extendHeight`.** They keep the uniform scale and grow the box to the
  window instead of stretching it. Content keeps its proportions and a flex layout spreads out.

```ts

const background = new SmartContainer({
    viewport: app.renderer,
    safeArea: { top: 44, bottom: 34 },
    breakpoints: [
        { name: 'ultrawide', when: (w, h) => w / h > 2.1, data: { width: 2560, height: 1080, viewportWidth: 2560, fitCover: true } },
    ],
    portraitData: { width: 1080, height: 1920, fitCover: true, ignoreSafeArea: true },
    landscapeData: { width: 1920, height: 1080, fitCover: true, ignoreSafeArea: true },
});
```

#### A flex layout inside the box

Without a `layout`, the children stay where you put them, in design units. Give the container a
`layout`, or a record its own, and the design box becomes a flex container:

```ts

const bar = new SmartContainer({
    viewport: app,
    layout: {
        padding: [16, 32, 28],
        gap: 24,
        alignItems: 'center',
        justifyContent: 'space-between',
        media: [{ breakpoint: 'portrait', style: { wrap: 'wrap', justifyContent: 'center' } }],
    },
    portraitData: { valign: 'bottom', width: 1080, height: 420, extendWidth: true },
    landscapeData: { valign: 'bottom', width: 1920, height: 200, extendWidth: true },
});
const spin = new Container();

bar.add(spin, { media: [{ breakpoint: 'portrait', style: { basis: '100%' } }] });
```

A `media` rule with `breakpoint` applies while that record is active. Everything below about
children, fit modes and text works the same inside a SmartContainer.

### Debug overlay

```ts

const debug = layoutDebug(app, { key: 'KeyL' });

debug.toggle();
```

`layoutDebug` draws over the whole stage and is off until you toggle it, with the key or the
handle. It shows, for each SmartContainer:

- the design box and a tag with the breakpoint and the scale,
- the reference resolution at the fit scale, in purple,
- the alignment anchor in the window, as a dot,
- the safe-area insets, in orange, and the stretched or extended axes, in yellow.

For each FlexContainer it shows the box, the content box, the padding in green and the gaps in
pink. Each child's slot is blue and its real bounds white, or red when the child overflows its slot.
Hover a box for a readout of its name, size, scale and alignment.

### Previewing devices

```ts

const sim = simulateViewport({ area: app.screen, device: 'phone portrait' });
const hud = new SmartContainer({ viewport: sim, portraitData: { width: 1080, height: 300, valign: 'bottom' } });

sim.content.addChild(hud);
app.stage.addChild(sim);
sim.setDevice('phone landscape');
```

`simulateViewport` shows a device window inside a desktop page, scaled down to fit and
letterboxed. Pass it as the `viewport` of your SmartContainers and add them to its `content`. They
lay out as they would on that device, so you can flip orientations without resizing the browser.
`DEVICES` lists the preset sizes. A `[width, height]` pair works too.

### FlexContainer

Live demo `layout-stack` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import type { AlignItems, FlexDirection, JustifyContent } from 'pixi-silk/layout';
import { SilkText } from 'pixi-silk/text';
import { FlexContainer, layoutDebug } from 'pixi-silk/ui/layout';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// An auto-layout stack, like a Figma frame. Change the item count, direction, gap, padding and
// alignment: cards glide to their new slots on springs. The debug overlay draws the boxes and
// slots (blue), the padding (green), the gaps (pink) and real bounds (white). Hover a card.

const COLORS = [0x0a84ff, 0x5e5ce6, 0xbf5af2, 0xff375f, 0xff9f0a, 0x30d158, 0x64d2ff, 0xffd60a];
const W = 860;
const H = 420;

/** A card of its own size that redraws at its slot size, so `align: stretch` shows. */
function card(frame: FlexContainer, i: number): Container {
    const c = new Container();
    const g = new SilkGraphics();
    const t = new SilkText({ text: String(i + 1), fontWeight: 700, fontSize: 22, fill: 0xffffff, anchor: 0.5 });

    c.addChild(g, t);

    return frame.add(c, {
        minWidth: 70 + ((i * 37) % 4) * 18,
        minHeight: 56 + ((i * 53) % 3) * 22,
        onSize: (w, h) => {
            g.clear()
                .roundRect(0, 0, w, h, 14, 0.6)
                .fill(COLORS[i % COLORS.length]);
            t.position.set(w / 2, h / 2);
        },
    });
}

export default defineDemo({
    size: [W, H],
    background: 0x151517,
    controls: {
        items: { type: 'range', label: 'items', min: 1, max: 14, step: 1, value: 6 },
        direction: {
            type: 'select',
            label: 'direction',
            options: ['row', 'column', 'row-reverse', 'column-reverse'],
            value: 'row',
        },
        wrap: { type: 'toggle', label: 'wrap', value: true },
        gap: { type: 'range', label: 'gap', min: 0, max: 40, step: 1, value: 12, unit: 'px' },
        padding: { type: 'range', label: 'padding', min: 0, max: 60, step: 1, value: 24, unit: 'px' },
        justify: {
            type: 'select',
            label: 'justify',
            options: ['start', 'center', 'end', 'space-between', 'space-around', 'space-evenly'],
            value: 'start',
        },
        align: { type: 'select', label: 'align', options: ['start', 'center', 'end', 'stretch'], value: 'start' },
        debug: { type: 'toggle', label: 'debug', value: true },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const frame = new FlexContainer({ ticker: silk.app.ticker, transition: true });
        // the layout debug overlay: boxes, padding, gaps, slots against real bounds. Hover a card.
        const debug = layoutDebug(silk.app, { enabled: true });
        const bg = new SilkGraphics();
        const cards: Container[] = [];
        let last = '';

        frame.resize(W, H);
        frame.add(bg, {
            position: 'absolute',
            inset: 0,
            animate: false,
            onSize: (w, h) => bg.clear().roundRect(0, 0, w, h, 20, 0.6).fill(0x1c1c1e),
        });
        stage.addChild(frame);

        tick(() => {
            const key = JSON.stringify(params);

            if (key === last) return;
            last = key;
            while (cards.length < params.items) cards.push(card(frame, cards.length));
            while (cards.length > params.items) cards.pop()?.destroy({ children: true });
            frame.setStyle({
                direction: params.direction as FlexDirection,
                wrap: params.wrap ? 'wrap' : 'nowrap',
                gap: params.gap,
                padding: params.padding,
                justifyContent: params.justify as JustifyContent,
                alignItems: params.align as AlignItems,
                alignContent: 'start',
            });
            if (debug.enabled !== params.debug) debug.toggle();
        });

        return () => {
            debug.destroy();
            frame.destroy({ children: true });
        };
    },
});
```

`FlexContainer` lays out its children with flexbox inside any box. The demo runs with the debug
overlay on. Hover a card to read its slot. Call `resize(width, height)` on
a root one, or nest it in another FlexContainer or a SmartContainer.

```ts

const row = new FlexContainer({ layout: { gap: 12, padding: 16, alignItems: 'center' } });

row.add(Sprite.from('coin.png'), { width: 48, height: 48, fit: 'contain' });
row.add(new Text({ text: 'Balance' }), { grow: 1 });
row.resize(480, 80);
```

Give a child a style with `add(child, style)` or `setLayout(child, style)`. Children with no style
take part with the defaults. A child is measured by its local bounds, or with the style's
`measure` (for wrapped text, use the `wrapText` helper). Pixi `Text` and `SilkText` both report real
text metrics.

The container lays out once per frame, and only after something changed. That is a child added,
removed or restyled, a new size, or `invalidate(child)` after new text or a new texture. Set
`watch: true` to check the children's bounds every frame instead. An idle container is off the
ticker and costs nothing.

With `transition`, children glide to their new slots on springs, scale included. A new child lands
in place. Set `animate: false` on a child to keep it from gliding.

A child with `onSize` is called with its slot size whenever that changes. Use it to draw a panel at
the size of its slot. Such a child measures as zero, so size it with `inset`, `grow` or a size:

```ts

const panel = new FlexContainer({ layout: { padding: 16 } });
const bg = new SilkGraphics();

panel.add(bg, {
    position: 'absolute',
    inset: 0,
    onSize: (w, h) => bg.clear().roundRect(0, 0, w, h, 18, 0.6).fill(0x1c1c1e),
});
```

#### Fit modes and text shrink-to-fit

Live demo `layout-fit` (source):

```ts
import type { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { SilkText } from 'pixi-silk/text';
import { type FitMode, FlexContainer } from 'pixi-silk/ui/layout';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// The same 200x120 artwork in five slots, one per fit mode, like CSS object-fit. Resize the
// slots and move the alignment. The last slot shrinks a long text to fit, down to a floor.

const MODES: FitMode[] = ['none', 'contain', 'cover', 'scale-down', 'fill'];
const ALIGN: Record<string, [number, number]> = { center: [0.5, 0.5], start: [0, 0], end: [1, 1] };

/** A 200x120 card: sky, sun and hills. */
function artwork(): Container {
    const g = new SilkGraphics();

    // everything stays inside the card, so its bounds are exactly 200x120
    g.roundRect(0, 0, 200, 120, 16, 0.6).fill(0x0a84ff);
    g.circle(150, 38, 18).fill(0xffd60a);
    g.poly([0, 84, 50, 62, 110, 86, 160, 70, 200, 82, 200, 104, 184, 120, 16, 120, 0, 104]).fill(0x30d158);

    return g;
}

export default defineDemo({
    size: [900, 360],
    background: 0x151517,
    controls: {
        width: { type: 'range', label: 'slot width', min: 60, max: 130, step: 1, value: 120, unit: 'px' },
        height: { type: 'range', label: 'slot height', min: 60, max: 220, step: 1, value: 180, unit: 'px' },
        align: { type: 'select', label: 'fitAlign', options: ['center', 'start', 'end'], value: 'center' },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const row = new FlexContainer({
            ticker: silk.app.ticker,
            layout: { gap: 14, padding: [24, 16], justifyContent: 'center', alignItems: 'start' },
        });
        const slots: { box: FlexContainer; art: Container }[] = [];
        const label = (text: string) => new SilkText({ text, fontSize: 14, fill: 0x8e8e93 });
        const slot = (title: string) => {
            const column = row.add(
                new FlexContainer({ layout: { direction: 'column', gap: 10, alignItems: 'center' } }),
            );
            const box = column.add(new FlexContainer());
            const frame = new SilkGraphics();
            const clip = new SilkGraphics();

            // the slot outline, drawn at its size
            box.add(frame, {
                position: 'absolute',
                inset: 0,
                onSize: (w, h) => {
                    frame.clear().roundRect(0, 0, w, h, 10).fill(0x232326).stroke({ width: 1, color: 0x48484a });
                    clip.clear().roundRect(0, 0, w, h, 10).fill(0xffffff);
                },
            });
            // the mask clips cover and fill to the slot. It goes on the slot, not on the child:
            // a masked child measures only what its mask shows.
            box.add(clip, { position: 'absolute', inset: 0, onSize: () => {} });
            box.mask = clip;
            column.add(label(title));

            return box;
        };

        for (const fit of MODES) {
            const box = slot(fit);

            slots.push({ box, art: box.add(artwork(), { fit }) });
        }
        // text shrink-to-fit: scale-down with a minimum scale
        const textBox = slot('scale-down text');
        const text = textBox.add(
            new SilkText({ text: 'MEGA WIN 1,250,000', fontWeight: 800, fontSize: 30, fill: 0xffd60a }),
            {
                fit: 'scale-down',
                minScale: 0.35,
            },
        );

        stage.addChild(row);
        row.resize(900, 360);

        let last = '';

        tick(() => {
            const key = JSON.stringify(params);

            if (key === last) return;
            last = key;
            const fitAlign = ALIGN[params.align];

            for (const { box, art } of slots) {
                box.setStyle({ width: params.width, height: params.height });
                box.setLayout(art, { ...box.getLayout(art), fitAlign });
            }
            textBox.setStyle({ width: params.width, height: params.height });
            textBox.setLayout(text, { ...textBox.getLayout(text), fitAlign });
        });

        return () => row.destroy({ children: true });
    },
});
```

`fit` scales a child into its slot, like CSS `object-fit`:

- **`none`** keeps the child's size. This is the default.
- **`contain`** fits it inside the slot.
- **`cover`** fills the slot and overflows. Mask the slot to clip it, not the child: a masked
  child measures only what its mask shows.
- **`scale-down`** is `contain` that never enlarges.
- **`fill`** stretches both axes.

`fitAlign` places the child in its slot, from `[0, 0]` (top left) to `[1, 1]` (bottom right). The
default is the centre. `minScale` and `maxScale` clamp the fit scale.

**Text shrink-to-fit** is `fit: 'scale-down'` with a floor. The text keeps its size while it fits
and scales down when its slot gets narrow, never below `minScale`:

```ts

const pill = new FlexContainer({ layout: { direction: 'column', padding: [10, 18] } });

pill.add(new SilkText({ text: '$1,250,000.00', fontSize: 30 }), { fit: 'scale-down', minScale: 0.55 });
```

#### Responsive styles

Any child style can carry `portrait` and `landscape` values, and `media` rules for widths and
breakpoints. Rules apply in order over the base values. Width and orientation rules test the root
container's layout area. A child with `display: 'none'` is hidden too.

```ts

const main = new FlexContainer({
    layout: {
        gap: 20,
        landscape: { direction: 'row' },
        portrait: { direction: 'column' },
        media: [{ maxWidth: 600, style: { gap: 10 } }],
    },
});
const banner = new FlexContainer();

main.add(banner, { landscape: { display: 'none' } });
```

### The flex engine

```ts

const root = new LayoutNode({ direction: 'row', gap: 8, padding: 16 });
const icon = new LayoutNode({ width: 100 });
const label = new LayoutNode({ grow: 1 });

root.add(icon).add(label);
root.calculate(400, 120);

label.layout; // { x: 124, y: 16, width: 260, height: 88 }
```

`pixi-silk/layout` is the engine under both containers. It works on plain nodes and has no Pixi
in it, so it runs in tests, workers and on a server. It is about 3.5 kB gzipped.

A node has a style, children and an optional `measure` function. `calculate(width, height)` lays out
the tree. Each node's `layout` holds its box relative to its parent. Leave a size out to fit that
axis to the content.

The style follows CSS flexbox, with `box-sizing: border-box` everywhere:

- **Container.** `direction` (`row`, `column` and their `-reverse`), `wrap`, `gap`, `rowGap`,
  `columnGap`, `padding`, `justifyContent`, `alignItems` and `alignContent`.
- **Item.** `grow`, `shrink`, `basis`, `alignSelf` and `margin`. An `auto` margin takes the free
  space, which centres an item or pushes it to the far end.
- **Size.** `width`, `height`, their `min` and `max`, and `aspectRatio`. Sizes are pixels, a percent
  of the parent's content box such as `'50%'`, or `'auto'`.
- **Position.** `position: 'absolute'` with `top`, `right`, `bottom`, `left` or `inset`. Anchors may
  be percents. `display: 'none'` takes a node out of the flow.

Grow and shrink run the CSS loop: items that hit their `min` or `max` freeze there, and the rest of
the space goes round again. Shrinking is weighted by the basis, as in a browser.

#### Measuring text

A leaf with content gets a `measure` function. It receives the space offered and returns the content
size. Either argument is `Infinity` when that axis is free.

```ts

const charWidth = 9;
const lineHeight = 20;
const copy = 'Free spins start on three scatters';

const text = new LayoutNode({}, (availableWidth) => {
    const perLine = Math.max(1, Math.floor(availableWidth / charWidth));
    const lines = Math.ceil(copy.length / perLine);

    return { width: Math.min(copy.length, perLine) * charWidth, height: lines * lineHeight };
});

new LayoutNode({ direction: 'column', width: 160 }).add(text).calculate();
```

Return a `baseline` too and `alignItems: 'baseline'` lines up text in a row.

#### Incremental layout

Changing a style, adding or removing a child, or calling `markDirty()` marks the node and its
ancestors dirty. The next `calculate()` skips every clean subtree whose space did not change. A
measured leaf keeps its sizes per available space, so it is measured again only after `markDirty()`.

### Differences from CSS

The engine follows CSS flexbox where results differ in practice. A few things are simpler:

- **Minimum size.** An item's default minimum is 0, not its content size. Set `minWidth` or
  `shrink: 0` to keep an item from shrinking below its content.
- **Aspect ratio and stretch.** As in CSS, a stretched cross size wins over `aspectRatio`. Set
  `alignSelf` to keep the ratio.
- **Baseline.** A container's baseline is its first item's. Only measured leaves report one of their
  own.
- **Absolute items** without anchors sit where the parent's `justifyContent` and `alignItems` put
  them, not at their static position.
- **Not there.** `order`, `overflow`, borders and `flex-basis: content` are not part of the engine.
  Rotated children keep their slot but are measured unrotated.

---

# Canvas and measurements

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/canvas-and-measurements/
Area: Editor UI

> Pan and zoom a PixiJS scene like a design tool, with pinch, fling and fit, and measure the distances between objects with redlines and size badges.

Demo sources run in the docs harness, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `editor-canvas` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { linear, SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { bindHistoryKeys, History, recordGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/history';
import { bindGizmo, bindMeasure, bindOutline, Selection } from 'pixi-silk/selection';
import { watch } from 'pixi-silk/signal';
import { SilkText, silkLabel } from 'pixi-silk/text';
import { Camera, ZoomControl } from 'pixi-silk/ui/camera';
import { TransformGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/gizmo';
import { altDown } from 'pixi-silk/ui/keys';
import { Measure } from 'pixi-silk/ui/measure';
import { SelectionOutline } from 'pixi-silk/ui/outline';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Scroll to pan, pinch or ctrl + scroll to zoom, right drag or Space + drag to pan.
// Click a layer to select it, Shift + click to add more. Hover another to measure the distance
// to it. Drag the arrows to move a layer, cmd or ctrl + Z undoes and Shift + cmd + Z redoes.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [760, 480],
    background: 0x151517,
    touchAction: 'none',
    ownsCamera: true,
    controls: {
        redlines: { type: 'select', label: 'redlines', options: ['on hover', 'with Alt'], value: 'on hover' },
    },
    setup({ silk, params }) {
        const app = silk.app;
        const world = new Container();
        const overlay = new Container();
        const camera = new Camera({ world, element: app.canvas });
        const hover = new SelectionOutline({ ticker: app.ticker });
        const outline = new SelectionOutline({ ticker: app.ticker });
        const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({ ticker: app.ticker });
        // SilkText labels: distance fields stay sharp at any zoom
        const label = silkLabel();
        const measure = new Measure({ space: world, ticker: app.ticker, label });
        const zoom = new ZoomControl(camera, { label });
        // one selection store drives the outlines, the gizmo and the redlines
        const selection = new Selection<Container>();
        const history = new History();

        gizmo.state.mode.set('move');
        overlay.addChild(hover, outline, gizmo, measure, zoom);
        app.stage.addChild(world, overlay);
        app.stage.eventMode = 'static';
        app.stage.hitArea = app.screen;
        app.stage.on('pointerdown', (e) => e.target === app.stage && !e.shiftKey && selection.clear());

        const redlines = () => params.redlines === 'on hover' || altDown();
        const measured = bindMeasure(selection, measure, { between: redlines });
        // while measuring, the hovered layer gets the red outline instead of the blue one
        const syncHover = () => {
            const over = selection.state.hovered.value;
            const quiet = !over || selection.has(over) || (redlines() && selection.primary);

            hover.hover(quiet ? null : over);
        };
        const stops = [
            bindOutline(selection, outline),
            watch(selection.state.hovered, syncHover),
            watch(selection.state.items, syncHover),
            bindGizmo(selection, gizmo),
            measured.stop,
            // every gizmo drag is one undo step, and the keys undo it
            recordGizmo(gizmo, history),
            bindHistoryKeys(history),
        ];

        // an artboard with a few layers on it
        const title = new SilkText({
            text: 'Home',
            fontFamily: 'Inter, system-ui, sans-serif',
            fontSize: 13,
            fill: 0x8e8e93,
        });

        title.position.set(0, -20);
        world.addChild(title);

        const layer = (x: number, y: number, draw: (g: SilkGraphics) => void) => {
            const item = new Container();
            const g = new SilkGraphics();

            draw(g);
            item.addChild(g);
            item.position.set(x, y);
            item.eventMode = 'static';
            item.cursor = 'pointer';
            item.on('pointerover', () => selection.state.hovered.set(item));
            item.on('pointerout', () => {
                if (selection.state.hovered.value === item) selection.state.hovered.set(null);
            });
```
