```ts
            item.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
                e.stopPropagation();
                selection.pick(item, e.shiftKey ? 'toggle' : 'replace');
            });
            world.addChild(item);

            return item;
        };

        const board = layer(0, 0, (g) => g.rect(0, 0, 390, 620).fill(0xf4f4f6));

        layer(24, 24, (g) =>
            g.roundRect(0, 0, 342, 180, 24, 0.6).fill(linear([0x5e5ce6, 0xbf5af2], { from: [0, 0], to: [1, 1] })),
        );
        const card = layer(24, 228, (g) =>
            g
                .roundRect(0, 0, 160, 120, 18, 0.6)
                .fill({ color: 0xffffff, innerShadow: { color: 0x000000, alpha: 0.06, y: -1, blur: 1 } }),
        );

        layer(206, 228, (g) => g.roundRect(0, 0, 160, 120, 18, 0.6).fill(0xffffff));
        layer(24, 372, (g) => g.roundRect(0, 0, 342, 64, 16, 0.6).fill(0xffffff));
        layer(48, 388, (g) => g.circle(16, 16, 16).fill(0x30d158));
        layer(24, 532, (g) => g.pill(0, 0, 342, 52).fill(0x1c1c1e));

        const place = () =>
            zoom.position.set(app.screen.width - zoom.size[0] - 12, app.screen.height - zoom.size[1] - 12);

        app.renderer.on('resize', place);
        place();
        selection.pick(card);
        camera.fit(board, 40, false);

        // Alt shows the redlines without moving the pointer
        const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => {
            if (e.key !== 'Alt') return;
            measured.sync();
            syncHover();
        };

        addEventListener('keydown', onKey);
        addEventListener('keyup', onKey);

        return () => {
            for (const stop of stops) stop();
            removeEventListener('keydown', onKey);
            removeEventListener('keyup', onKey);
            camera.destroy();
        };
    },
});
```

### Camera

`Camera` pans and zooms a world container over an infinite canvas. It works the way design tools
do, so people don't have to learn it.

- A trackpad pinch, or ctrl or cmd with the wheel, zooms around the cursor. Scrolling pans.
- Middle drag, right drag and Space + drag pan. On touch screens two fingers pinch and pan.
- A fling keeps gliding and slows down.
- `+` and `-` zoom, Shift + 0 is 100% and Shift + 1 fits the content.

Pans never reach the scene, so your own clicks and drags keep working. Silk redraws its shapes
exactly at every zoom, so they stay sharp from 2% to as far as you let it go.

```ts

const world = new Container();
const overlay = new Container();
const camera = new Camera({ world, element: app.canvas });

app.stage.addChild(world, overlay);
overlay.addChild(new ZoomControl(camera));
camera.fit();
```

Put overlays beside the world, not inside it. Outlines, gizmos and measurements on the overlay
follow the objects in the world and keep their size on screen.

`fit()`, `zoomTo()`, `zoomBy()` and `centerOn()` animate by default. `state.zoom`, `state.x` and
`state.y` are signals, for readouts and for anything that redraws on zoom. `screenToWorld()` and
`worldToScreen()` convert points.

Pass `wheel: 'zoom'` for map-style scrolling, where the wheel zooms and Shift + wheel pans.

In a page that scrolls, a plain scroll over the canvas keeps scrolling the page until the canvas is
clicked, so an embedded editor never traps the reader. Pinch and ctrl + scroll zoom at once.
`pageScroll: false` gives the canvas every scroll. Full-screen canvases get them by default.

`fit()` takes padding per side, like `{ left: 280, right: 300 }`, to centre content between docked panels.

### Measurements

`Measure` draws the distances from one object to another, the way Figma does when you hold Alt.

- Two objects side by side: the gap between them, along the band they share.
- Objects that don't line up: the gap, plus a dashed guide to where the other edge would be.
- One inside the other: the margins on every side.

`size()` puts a `W x H` badge under an object. Both follow their objects every frame, so they
stay right while things move.

```ts

declare const world: Container, overlay: Container, selected: Container, hovered: Container;

const measure = new Measure({ space: world });

overlay.addChild(measure);
measure.size(selected);
measure.between(selected, hovered);
```

Distances are measured in `space`, usually the camera's world. A gap of 24 reads 24 at any zoom.
`format` turns values into labels, for units or rounding.

`Measure` and `ZoomControl` draw their text with PixiJS `Text` by default. Pass
`label: silkLabel()` from `pixi-silk/text` to draw it with SilkText instead, which stays sharp at
any zoom and resolution. The widgets never import SilkText on their own, so apps that skip the
option do not ship it.

```ts

declare const camera: Camera, world: Container;

const label = silkLabel();
const measure = new Measure({ space: world, label });
const zoom = new ZoomControl(camera, { label });
```

---

# Grid, rulers and selection

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/canvas-tools/
Area: Editor UI

> Add a zoom-aware grid, rulers, draggable guides, rubber-band selection, group transforms and Figma-style smart spacing to a PixiJS canvas.

Demo sources run in the docs harness, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `canvas-tools` (source):

```ts
import { Container, type FederatedPointerEvent } from 'pixi.js';
import { linear, SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { silkLabel } from 'pixi-silk/text';
import { Camera, ZoomControl } from 'pixi-silk/ui/camera';
import { TransformGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/gizmo';
import { CanvasGrid } from 'pixi-silk/ui/grid';
import { SelectionGroup } from 'pixi-silk/ui/group';
import { Guides } from 'pixi-silk/ui/guides';
import { altDown } from 'pixi-silk/ui/keys';
import { Marquee } from 'pixi-silk/ui/marquee';
import { Measure } from 'pixi-silk/ui/measure';
import { boundsQuad, SelectionOutline, unionQuad } from 'pixi-silk/ui/outline';
import { Ruler } from 'pixi-silk/ui/ruler';
import { SnapEngine, type SnapRect } from 'pixi-silk/ui/snap';
import { findSpacing, SpacingMarkers } from 'pixi-silk/ui/spacing';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Drag on empty canvas to select, Shift adds and Alt takes away. Drag selected layers to move
// them: pink markers show equal gaps. Drag guides out of the rulers, drop them back to remove.
// Scroll to pan, pinch or ctrl + scroll to zoom.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [760, 520],
    background: 0x151517,
    touchAction: 'none',
    ownsCamera: true,
    controls: {
        grid: { type: 'select', label: 'grid', options: ['lines', 'dots', 'off'], value: 'lines' },
        marquee: { type: 'select', label: 'marquee', options: ['intersect', 'contain'], value: 'intersect' },
    },
    setup({ silk, params }) {
        const app = silk.app;
        const world = new Container();
        const overlay = new Container();
        const camera = new Camera({ world, element: app.canvas });
        const grid = new CanvasGrid({ camera, ticker: app.ticker });
        // SilkText labels: distance fields stay sharp at any zoom
        const label = silkLabel();
        const ruler = new Ruler({ camera, ticker: app.ticker, label });
        const engine = new SnapEngine();
        const guides = new Guides({ camera, ruler, snap: engine, ticker: app.ticker, label });
        const group = new SelectionGroup();
        const hover = new SelectionOutline({ ticker: app.ticker });
        const outline = new SelectionOutline({ ticker: app.ticker, brackets: false });
        const spacing = new SpacingMarkers({ space: world, ticker: app.ticker });
        const measure = new Measure({ space: world, ticker: app.ticker, label });
        const layers: Container[] = [];
        let selected: readonly Container[] = [];
        let hovered: Container | null = null;

        const rectOf = (items: readonly Container[]): SnapRect => {
            const q = unionQuad(items.map((t) => boundsQuad(t, world)));

            return { x: q[0].x, y: q[0].y, width: q[2].x - q[0].x, height: q[2].y - q[0].y };
        };
        const others = () => layers.filter((l) => !selected.includes(l)).map((l) => rectOf([l]));
        const hooks = group.hooks();
        const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({
            mode: 'box',
            snapEngine: engine,
            ticker: app.ticker,
            onStart: () => {
                hooks.onStart();
                // snap to everything that is not moving, and to the guides
                engine.targets = others();
            },
            onChange: (_t, handle) => {
                hooks.onChange();
                if (handle === 'box-move') spacing.show(findSpacing(rectOf(selected), engine.targets));
            },
            onEnd: () => {
                hooks.onEnd();
                spacing.clear();
            },
        });
        const marquee = new Marquee({
            camera,
            surface: app.stage,
            candidates: () => layers,
            selection: () => selected,
            ticker: app.ticker,
            // live while dragging: only the outline follows, the gizmo comes when it ends
            onChange: (list) => (marquee.state.active.value ? preview(list) : select(list)),
            onEnd: (list) => select(list),
        });
        const zoom = new ZoomControl(camera, { label });

        world.addChild(group);
        guides.bindSnap(engine);
        camera.state.zoom.subscribe((k) => {
            engine.threshold = 6 / k;
        });
        engine.threshold = 6 / camera.zoom;
        overlay.addChild(hover, outline, spacing, measure, gizmo, guides, marquee, ruler, zoom);
        app.stage.addChild(grid, world, overlay);
        app.stage.eventMode = 'static';
        app.stage.hitArea = app.screen;

        function preview(list: readonly Container[]) {
            selected = list;
            outline.select(list);
            ruler.highlight(list.length ? list : null);
        }
        function select(list: readonly Container[]) {
            preview(list);
            // the gizmo's box frames the selection from here on
            outline.select([]);
            group.set(list);
            gizmo.attach(group.target);
            measure.size(list.length ? group.target : null);
            sync();
        }
        const sync = () => {
            const pair = altDown() && selected.length === 1 && hovered && hovered !== selected[0];

            measure.between(pair ? selected[0] : null, pair ? hovered : null);
            hover.hover(pair || (hovered && selected.includes(hovered)) ? null : hovered);
        };

        // an artboard: a frame you can marquee across, with layers on it
        const board = new SilkGraphics().rect(0, 0, 480, 360).fill(0xf4f4f6);

        world.addChildAt(board, 0);
        const layer = (x: number, y: number, draw: (g: SilkGraphics) => void) => {
            const item = new Container();
            const g = new SilkGraphics();

            draw(g);
            item.addChild(g);
            item.position.set(x, y);
            item.eventMode = 'static';
            item.cursor = 'pointer';
            item.on('pointerover', () => {
                hovered = item;
                sync();
            });
            item.on('pointerout', () => {
                if (hovered === item) hovered = null;
                sync();
            });
            item.on('pointerdown', (e: FederatedPointerEvent) => {
                if (e.button !== 0) return;
                e.stopPropagation();
                if (e.shiftKey)
                    select(selected.includes(item) ? selected.filter((s) => s !== item) : [...selected, item]);
                else if (!selected.includes(item)) select([item]);
                // press and move in one go
                if (selected.includes(item)) gizmo.grab(e);
            });
            world.addChildAt(item, world.children.length - 1);
            layers.push(item);

            return item;
        };

        layer(32, 32, (g) =>
            g.roundRect(0, 0, 416, 120, 24, 0.6).fill(linear([0x5e5ce6, 0xbf5af2], { from: [0, 0], to: [1, 1] })),
        );
        const a = layer(32, 184, (g) => g.roundRect(0, 0, 120, 96, 18, 0.6).fill(0xffffff));

        layer(180, 184, (g) => g.roundRect(0, 0, 120, 96, 18, 0.6).fill(0xffffff));
        layer(360, 184, (g) => g.roundRect(0, 0, 88, 96, 18, 0.6).fill(0xffffff));
        layer(32, 304, (g) => g.circle(12, 12, 12).fill(0x30d158));
        layer(72, 308, (g) => g.pill(0, 0, 160, 16).fill(0x1c1c1e));

        guides.add('x', 32);
        guides.add('y', 184);

        const place = () =>
            zoom.position.set(app.screen.width - zoom.size[0] - 12, app.screen.height - zoom.size[1] - 12);

        app.renderer.on('resize', place);
        place();
        select([a]);
        camera.fit({ x: -40, y: -40, width: 560, height: 440 }, 24, false);

        const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => e.key === 'Alt' && sync();

        addEventListener('keydown', onKey);
        addEventListener('keyup', onKey);
        app.ticker.add(() => {
            grid.state.visible.set(params.grid !== 'off');
            if (params.grid !== 'off') grid.state.style.set(params.grid as 'lines' | 'dots');
            marquee.mode = params.marquee as 'intersect' | 'contain';
        });

        return () => {
            removeEventListener('keydown', onKey);
            removeEventListener('keyup', onKey);
            camera.destroy();
            ruler.destroy();
            marquee.destroy();
        };
    },
});
```

These are the pieces around a design tool's canvas. Each one is its own entry point and works with
the [camera](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/canvas-and-measurements/). They draw in screen space, so lines stay one pixel
wide at any zoom. Every one works with a mouse, a pen and touch.

None of them holds a selection. They take plain arrays of containers and report back through
callbacks, so any selection store can drive them.

### Grid

`CanvasGrid` draws an infinite grid behind the world. Levels nest, 1, 10 and 100 world units by
default, and each one fades in as its lines spread apart on screen. Zooming never pops a level in
or out.

```ts

const world = new Container();
const camera = new Camera({ world, element: app.canvas });
const grid = new CanvasGrid({ camera, style: 'dots', axes: true });

app.stage.addChild(grid, world);
```

All vertical lines of a level are one dashed stroke, and so are the horizontal ones. A level costs
two silk shapes, whatever the size of the view. Dots take one shape per row. `state.style`,
`state.axes` and `state.visible` are signals, and changes crossfade.

`ratio` sets how much each level grows, `unit` the finest step and `fade` the screen spacing where
a level starts and finishes fading in. `gridLevels()` returns the levels for a zoom, for your own
drawing.

Live demo `canvas-grid` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { Camera, ZoomControl } from 'pixi-silk/ui/camera';
import { CanvasGrid } from 'pixi-silk/ui/grid';
import { Ruler } from 'pixi-silk/ui/ruler';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Pinch or ctrl + scroll to zoom: grid levels fade in and out, ruler labels step in 1, 2, 5.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [760, 420],
    background: 0x151517,
    touchAction: 'none',
    ownsCamera: true,
    controls: {
        style: { type: 'select', label: 'grid', options: ['lines', 'dots'], value: 'lines' },
        axes: { type: 'toggle', label: 'axes', value: true },
    },
    setup({ silk, params }) {
        const app = silk.app;
        const world = new Container();
        const overlay = new Container();
        const camera = new Camera({ world, element: app.canvas, zoomRange: [0.05, 64] });
        const grid = new CanvasGrid({ camera, ticker: app.ticker });
        const ruler = new Ruler({ camera, ticker: app.ticker });
        const zoom = new ZoomControl(camera);
        const card = new SilkGraphics().roundRect(0, 0, 200, 120, 20, 0.6).fill(0x0a84ff);

        card.position.set(40, 40);
        world.addChild(card);
        // the ruler bands the card's extent
        ruler.highlight(card);
        overlay.addChild(ruler, zoom);
        app.stage.addChild(grid, world, overlay);

        const place = () =>
            zoom.position.set(app.screen.width - zoom.size[0] - 12, app.screen.height - zoom.size[1] - 12);

        app.renderer.on('resize', place);
        place();
        camera.fit({ x: -40, y: -40, width: 320, height: 240 }, 24, false);
        app.ticker.add(() => {
            grid.state.style.set(params.style as 'lines' | 'dots');
            grid.state.axes.set(params.axes);
        });

        return () => {
            camera.destroy();
            ruler.destroy();
            grid.destroy();
        };
    },
});
```

### Rulers

`Ruler` draws a top and a left ruler. Labels step in 1, 2 and 5 times a power of ten, and the ones
a new step shares with the old one stay put while the rest fade. A marker follows the pointer.

```ts

declare const camera: Camera, overlay: Container, selected: Container[];

const ruler = new Ruler({ camera, format: (v) => `${Math.round(v)}` });

overlay.addChild(ruler);
ruler.highlight(selected);
```

`highlight()` takes objects or a world rectangle. The ruler bands their extent and labels its
edges in the accent colour, following the objects every frame. `format` turns world values into
labels, for units such as `pt` or `mm`. Pass `label: silkLabel()` from `pixi-silk/text` to draw
the labels with SilkText, which stays sharp at any resolution. `Guides` takes the same option for
its pill.

### Guides

`Guides` adds guide lines you drag out of a ruler. The top ruler gives a horizontal guide, the
left ruler a vertical one. Drag a guide to move it, or drop it back on the ruler to remove it. A
pill shows its position while you drag.

```ts

declare const camera: Camera, overlay: Container, ruler: Ruler;

const engine = new SnapEngine();
const guides = new Guides({ camera, ruler, snap: engine });

overlay.addChild(guides, ruler);
guides.bindSnap(engine);
guides.state.guides.subscribe((list) => console.log(list));
```

While you drag, a guide snaps to the edges and centres of the engine's targets. `bindSnap()` feeds
the guides back into the engine, so objects snap to them too. Ctrl or Cmd turns snapping off for
a drag. `add()`, `move()`, `remove()` and `clear()` edit the guides from code.

### Marquee

`Marquee` draws a rubber-band rectangle when a left drag starts on empty canvas. Shift adds to the
selection, Alt takes away and Escape cancels. The camera keeps middle, right, Space and
two-finger drags, and a pan that starts mid-drag cancels the marquee.

```ts

declare const camera: Camera, overlay: Container, layers: Container[];
let selected: readonly Container[] = [];

app.stage.eventMode = 'static';
app.stage.hitArea = app.screen;
const marquee = new Marquee({
    camera,
    surface: app.stage,
    candidates: () => layers,
    selection: () => selected,
    onChange: (next) => {
        selected = next;
    },
});

overlay.addChild(marquee);
```

`mode: 'intersect'` picks what the rectangle touches, rotated objects included. `mode: 'contain'`
picks what is fully inside. For a spatial index or custom shapes, pass `hit: (rect) => targets`
instead of `candidates`. Near the edge of the view the camera pans.

### Multi-select

`SelectionGroup` lets the `TransformGizmo` move, resize and rotate several objects at once. It is
an invisible box around them, and every change of the box applies to each object about the common
pivot.

```ts

declare const world: Container, overlay: Container, selected: Container[];

const group = new SelectionGroup();
const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({ mode: 'box', ...group.hooks() });

world.addChild(group);
overlay.addChild(gizmo);
group.set(selected);
gizmo.attach(group.target);
```

`group.target` is the object itself when one is selected, so it keeps its own turned frame. For
several it is the group. Objects can have different parents. After objects move on their own,
call `refit()`.

### Smart spacing

`findSpacing()` looks at a box you drag among others, the way Figma does. When the gap to a
neighbour equals another gap in the same row or column, it returns every one of those gaps. It
also returns edges and centres that line up. `SpacingMarkers` draws them as pink segments with
value pills and thin alignment lines.

```ts

declare const world: Container, overlay: Container, moving: SnapRect, others: SnapRect[];

const engine = new SnapEngine({ targets: others });
const markers = new SpacingMarkers({ space: world });

overlay.addChild(markers);
const snap = engine.snap(moving);
const snapped = { ...moving, x: moving.x + snap.dx, y: moving.y + snap.dy };

markers.show(findSpacing(snapped, others));
```

Snap with the `SnapEngine` first, then report on the snapped box. `gapsBetween()` reads the gaps of
a row or a column, so an auto-layout container can show its spacing with the same markers.
`spacingFromSnap()` draws a snap result's own guides.

Live demo `smart-spacing` (source):

```ts
import { Container, type FederatedPointerEvent, type PointData } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { SnapEngine, type SnapRect } from 'pixi-silk/ui/snap';
import { findSpacing, gapsBetween, SpacingMarkers } from 'pixi-silk/ui/spacing';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Drag a card: it snaps to equal gaps and shared edges, and pink markers show every equal gap.
// Hover the stack on the right to see its spacing, the way an auto-layout frame shows it.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [760, 380],
    background: 0x151517,
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        snap: { type: 'toggle', label: 'snap', value: true },
    },
    setup({ stage, params }) {
        const scene = new Container();
        const markers = new SpacingMarkers({ space: scene });
        const stack = new Container();
        const engine = new SnapEngine({ threshold: 8 });
        const cards: Container[] = [];
        const rect = (c: Container): SnapRect => {
            const b = c.getLocalBounds();

            return { x: c.x + b.minX, y: c.y + b.minY, width: b.width, height: b.height };
        };
        let drag: { card: Container; grab: PointData; id: number } | null = null;

        stage.addChild(scene, markers);
        stage.eventMode = 'static';
        stage.hitArea = { contains: () => true };

        const card = (x: number, y: number, w: number, h: number, color: number) => {
            const c = new Container();

            c.addChild(new SilkGraphics().roundRect(0, 0, w, h, 14, 0.6).fill(color));
            c.position.set(x, y);
            c.eventMode = 'static';
            c.cursor = 'grab';
            c.on('pointerdown', (e: FederatedPointerEvent) => {
                const p = scene.toLocal(e.global);

                drag = { card: c, grab: { x: p.x - c.x, y: p.y - c.y }, id: e.pointerId };
                engine.targets = cards.filter((o) => o !== c).map(rect);
            });
            scene.addChild(c);
            cards.push(c);

            return c;
        };

        card(40, 60, 110, 80, 0x0a84ff);
        card(190, 60, 110, 80, 0x5e5ce6);
        card(380, 80, 90, 60, 0xbf5af2);
        card(110, 220, 140, 90, 0x30d158);

        stage.on('globalpointermove', (e: FederatedPointerEvent) => {
            if (!drag || e.pointerId !== drag.id) return;
            const p = scene.toLocal(e.global);
            const c = drag.card;
            const b = c.getLocalBounds();
            const moving = {
                x: p.x - drag.grab.x + b.minX,
                y: p.y - drag.grab.y + b.minY,
                width: b.width,
                height: b.height,
            };
            const snap = params.snap ? engine.snap(moving) : { dx: 0, dy: 0 };

            c.position.set(p.x - drag.grab.x + snap.dx, p.y - drag.grab.y + snap.dy);
            // what lines up after snapping
            markers.show(findSpacing(rect(c), engine.targets));
        });
        const end = () => {
            drag = null;
            markers.clear();
        };

        stage.on('pointerup', end);
        stage.on('pointerupoutside', end);

        // an auto-layout stack: its children's gaps on hover
        stack.position.set(560, 40);
        for (const [i, h] of [56, 40, 72].entries()) {
            const row = new SilkGraphics().roundRect(0, 0, 150, h, 10, 0.6).fill(0x2c2c2e);

            row.y = [0, 72, 128][i];
            stack.addChild(row);
        }
        stack.eventMode = 'static';
        stack.on('pointerover', () => {
            const rows = stack.children.map((r) => ({ x: stack.x, y: stack.y + r.y, width: 150, height: r.height }));

            markers.show({ gaps: gapsBetween(rows, 'y') });
        });
        stack.on('pointerout', () => markers.clear());
        scene.addChild(stack);

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

---

# Transform gizmos

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/transform-gizmos/
Area: Editor UI

> Move, rotate, scale and bounding-box handles with snapping, selection outlines that follow rotated objects, and smart guides for edges, centres and gaps.

Demo sources run in the docs harness, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `gizmo` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { type GizmoMode, type GizmoSpace, TransformGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/gizmo';
import { SelectionOutline } from 'pixi-silk/ui/outline';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Click a shape to select it, then drag the handles. Hold Shift to snap.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [640, 420],
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        mode: {
            type: 'select',
            label: 'mode',
            options: ['universal', 'move', 'rotate', 'scale', 'box'],
            value: 'universal',
        },
        space: { type: 'select', label: 'space', options: ['local', 'world'], value: 'local' },
        snap: { type: 'toggle', label: 'always snap', value: false },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const scene = new Container();
        const overlay = new Container();
        const background = new Container();
        const hover = new SelectionOutline({ ticker });
        const selection = new SelectionOutline({ ticker });
        const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({ ticker });

        background.eventMode = 'static';
        background.hitArea = { contains: () => true };
        background.on('pointerdown', () => {
            gizmo.attach(null);
            selection.select(null);
        });
        stage.addChild(background, scene, overlay);
        overlay.addChild(hover, selection, gizmo);

        const shape = (x: number, y: number, rotation: number, draw: (g: SilkGraphics) => void) => {
            const item = new Container();
            const g = new SilkGraphics();

            draw(g);
            item.addChild(g);
            item.position.set(x, y);
            item.rotation = rotation;
            item.eventMode = 'static';
            item.cursor = 'pointer';
            item.on('pointerover', () => hover.hover(item));
            item.on('pointerout', () => hover.hover(null));
            item.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
                e.stopPropagation();
                selection.select(item);
                gizmo.attach(item);
            });
            scene.addChild(item);

            return item;
        };

        const card = shape(190, 210, -0.2, (g) => g.roundRect(-70, -45, 140, 90, 18, 0.6).fill(0x30d158));

        shape(400, 150, 0.3, (g) => g.star(0, 0, 5, 52, undefined, 0, 6).fill(0xffd60a));
        shape(460, 300, 0, (g) => g.roundRect(-55, -30, 110, 60, 30).fill(0xbf5af2));
        selection.select(card);
        gizmo.attach(card);

        tick(() => {
            gizmo.state.mode.set(params.mode as GizmoMode);
            gizmo.state.space.set(params.space as GizmoSpace);
            // the box mode draws its own frame
            selection.alpha = params.mode === 'box' ? 0 : 1;
            gizmo.snap.enabled = params.snap;
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

### Three pieces

```ts
```

Each one works alone. Put the outline and the gizmo in an overlay above your scene: they read the
target's transform every frame, so they follow it wherever it sits in the tree.

### Transform gizmo

```ts

const overlay = new Container();
const selectedShape = new Container();
const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({
    mode: 'universal', // 'move' | 'rotate' | 'scale' | 'box' | 'universal'
    space: 'local', // move along the object's own axes, or 'world'
    snap: { translate: 10, angle: Math.PI / 12, scale: 0.1 },
    onChange: (t) => console.log(t.x, t.y, t.rotation, t.scaleX, t.scaleY),
});

overlay.addChild(gizmo);
gizmo.attach(selectedShape);
gizmo.state.mode.set('rotate');
```

- **Move.** The arrows lock to one axis, the centre square moves freely.
- **Rotate.** The ring turns the object about its pivot. The swept angle is shaded and read out
  in a pill, and snap ticks appear while snapping.
- **Scale.** Cube handles stretch one axis, the centre ring (or the corner cube in universal mode)
  scales evenly. Scale handles follow the object's own axes in both spaces.
- **Box.** Eight handles resize in the object's own frame while the opposite side stays put.
  Shift keeps the aspect, Alt resizes from the centre. The zones right outside the corners rotate
  the box about its centre.

Moving the gizmo to another object pops it. The old handles shrink away where they were, and the
new ones spring in group by group: arrows first, then the ring, then the scale handles. The gizmo
ignores the pointer until they land. `transition: 'glide'` slides the handles over instead.

Shift snaps moves to the grid, angles to 15 degrees and scales to steps. Set `snap.enabled` to snap
by default, and Shift then turns it off. Handles light up under the pointer, step back while
another one is dragged, and grow under a finger.

The gizmo writes the new transform into the target. Pass `apply: false` to handle it yourself in
`onChange`, for undo stacks or networked editors. `gizmo.grab(event)` starts a move from a press
on the object itself, so a click on an unselected shape can select and drag it in one gesture.

The box handles assume the object is not mirrored (positive scales).

### Selection outline

```ts

const [shape, shapeA, shapeB] = [new Container(), new Container(), new Container()];
const hover = new SelectionOutline();
const selection = new SelectionOutline({ dash: [5, 4], march: 16, padding: 4 });

shape.on('pointerover', () => hover.hover(shape));
shape.on('pointerout', () => hover.hover(null));
selection.select([shapeA, shapeB]); // one box around both
```

The hover style is a quiet line. The selected style has marching dashes and corner brackets.
Rotated objects get a rotated box, several objects one box around all of them. The frame follows
its objects exactly while they move.

When the selection changes, the old frame shrinks and fades where it was, its dashes and brackets
retracting, while the new frame springs in around the new objects. Pass `transition: 'glide'` to
slide one frame over instead.

### Smart guides

Live demo `snap` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { TransformGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/gizmo';
import { boundsQuad, SelectionOutline } from 'pixi-silk/ui/outline';
import { SnapEngine, SnapGuides } from 'pixi-silk/ui/snap';

import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Drag a card: edges and centres line up with the others, and equal gaps show their size.
// Hold Ctrl or Cmd to move without guides.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [640, 380],
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        spacing: { type: 'toggle', label: 'equal spacing', value: true },
        threshold: { type: 'range', label: 'threshold', min: 2, max: 16, step: 1, value: 6 },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const scene = new Container();
        const engine = new SnapEngine({ lines: { x: [320], y: [190] } });
        const guides = new SnapGuides({ ticker });
        const selection = new SelectionOutline({ ticker });
        const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({ ticker, mode: 'box', snapEngine: engine, snapGuides: guides });
        const cards: Container[] = [];
        const colors = [0x0a84ff, 0xff9f0a, 0x30d158, 0xbf5af2];
        const select = (card: Container | null) => {
            selection.select(card);
            gizmo.attach(card);
        };

        stage.eventMode = 'static';
        stage.hitArea = { contains: () => true };
        stage.on('pointerdown', () => select(null));
        stage.addChild(scene, guides, selection, gizmo);
        [
            [70, 80],
            [250, 80],
            [430, 120],
            [140, 240],
        ].forEach(([x, y], i) => {
            const card = new Container();
            const g = new SilkGraphics();

            g.roundRect(0, 0, i === 2 ? 140 : 110, 80, 14, 0.6).fill(colors[i]);
            card.addChild(g);
            card.position.set(x, y);
            card.eventMode = 'static';
            card.cursor = 'move';
            card.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
                e.stopPropagation();
                select(card);
                // press and drag in one go, even on a card that was not selected yet
                gizmo.grab(e);
            });
            scene.addChild(card);
            cards.push(card);
        });
        select(cards[3]);

        // what to snap against: every card, read as rectangles in the scene's space
        const refresh = () => {
            engine.targets = cards.map((c) => {
                const q = boundsQuad(c, scene);
                const xs = q.map((p) => p.x);
                const ys = q.map((p) => p.y);

                return {
                    x: Math.min(...xs),
                    y: Math.min(...ys),
                    width: Math.max(...xs) - Math.min(...xs),
                    height: Math.max(...ys) - Math.min(...ys),
                };
            });
        };

        gizmo.state.dragging.subscribe((on) => on && refresh());
        refresh();
        tick(() => {
            engine.spacing = params.spacing;
            engine.threshold = params.threshold;
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

`SnapEngine` is plain maths with no drawing. Give it rectangles, guide lines and points, and ask
where a moving rectangle should go:

```ts

const otherRects = [{ x: 0, y: 0, width: 100, height: 60 }];
const engine = new SnapEngine({ threshold: 6, targets: otherRects, lines: { x: [320] } });
const box = { x: 103, y: 40, width: 80, height: 60 };
const r = engine.snap(box);

box.x += r.dx;
box.y += r.dy;
```

It aligns edges with edges and centres with centres, and also offers equal spacing: centred
between two neighbours, or at a gap that already exists in the row. `SnapGuides` draws the
result, with dashed lines and gap sizes in pills. Each alignment fades in when found and out when
lost, so guides never slide from one alignment to the next. Hand both to a gizmo with `snapEngine` and
`snapGuides` and its free moves snap on their own.

---

# Selection and undo

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/selection-and-history/
Area: Editor UI

> Track what is selected, hovered and primary in a PixiJS editor, and make every edit undoable with transactions, merging, signal recording and keyboard shortcuts.

Demo sources run in the docs harness, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `selection-history` (source):

```ts
import { Container, Text } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { bindHistoryKeys, History, recordGizmo, recordSignals } from 'pixi-silk/history';
import { bindGizmo, bindOutline, Selection } from 'pixi-silk/selection';
import { type Signal, signal, watch } from 'pixi-silk/signal';
import { SilkButton } from 'pixi-silk/ui/button';
import { TransformGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/gizmo';
import { SelectionOutline } from 'pixi-silk/ui/outline';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

const COLORS = [0x0a84ff, 0x30d158, 0xff9f0a, 0xff375f, 0xbf5af2, 0x64d2ff];

// Click a shape, Shift + click to add more, then drag the gizmo. Circles selects by a test.
// Tint recolours the selection through signals, recorded without writing a command.
// Every step lands in the list on the right. Cmd or ctrl + Z undoes, Shift + cmd + Z redoes.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [760, 420],
    background: 0x151517,
    touchAction: 'none',
    setup({ stage, silk }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const scene = new Container();
        const overlay = new Container();
        const history = new History();
        const selection = new Selection<Container>();
        const outline = new SelectionOutline({ ticker });
        const hover = new SelectionOutline({ ticker });
        const gizmo = new TransformGizmo({ ticker, readout: false });
        const colors = new Map<Container, Signal<number>>();

        overlay.addChild(hover, outline, gizmo);
        stage.addChild(scene, overlay);
        stage.eventMode = 'static';
        stage.hitArea = { contains: (x: number, y: number) => x >= 0 && y >= 0 && x <= 760 && y <= 420 };
        // a press on the background (not on a button or a shape) clears the selection
        stage.on('pointerdown', (e) => e.target === stage && !e.shiftKey && selection.clear());

        const shape = (kind: 'circle' | 'square', x: number, y: number, i: number) => {
            const item = new Container({ label: kind });
            const g = new SilkGraphics();
            const color = signal(COLORS[i % COLORS.length]);

            watch(color, (c) => {
                g.clear();
                if (kind === 'circle') g.circle(0, 0, 38).fill(c);
                else g.roundRect(-36, -36, 72, 72, 18, 0.6).fill(c);
            });
            item.addChild(g);
            item.position.set(x, y);
            item.eventMode = 'static';
            item.cursor = 'pointer';
            item.on('pointerover', () => selection.state.hovered.set(item));
            item.on('pointerout', () => selection.state.hovered.set(null));
            item.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
                e.stopPropagation();
                selection.pick(item, e.shiftKey ? 'toggle' : 'replace');
                // press and drag in one go
                if (!e.shiftKey) gizmo.grab(e);
            });
            scene.addChild(item);
            colors.set(item, color);

            return item;
        };
        const items = [
            shape('circle', 110, 150, 0),
            shape('square', 240, 150, 1),
            shape('circle', 370, 150, 2),
            shape('square', 110, 290, 3),
            shape('circle', 240, 290, 4),
            shape('square', 370, 290, 5),
        ];
        const stops = [
            bindOutline(selection, outline, hover),
            bindGizmo(selection, gizmo),
            // one undo step per drag, and colour changes recorded from the signals
            recordGizmo(gizmo, history),
            recordSignals(history, [...colors.values()], { label: 'Tint' }).stop,
            bindHistoryKeys(history),
        ];

        // buttons
        const dark = { fill: 0x2c2c2e, fillHover: 0x3a3a3c, fillPress: 0x1c1c1e, fillDisabled: 0x232325 };
        let tint = 0;
        const button = (label: string, x: number, onPress: () => void) => {
            const b = new SilkButton({ ticker, label, width: 84, height: 32, theme: dark, onPress });

            b.position.set(x, 40);
            overlay.addChild(b);

            return b;
        };

        button('Circles', 66, () => selection.selectWhere(items, (s) => s.label === 'circle'));
        button('Tint', 160, () => {
            tint++;
            // one handler, one step: every colour set here undoes together
            for (const item of selection.items)
                colors.get(item)?.set(COLORS[(COLORS.indexOf(colors.get(item)?.value ?? 0) + tint) % COLORS.length]);
        });
        const undo = button('Undo', 254, () => history.undo());
        const redo = button('Redo', 348, () => history.redo());

        stops.push(
            watch(history.state.canUndo, (v) => undo.state.enabled.set(v)),
            watch(history.state.canRedo, (v) => redo.state.enabled.set(v)),
        );

        // the stack, newest at the bottom, undone steps greyed out
        const panel = new SilkGraphics();
        const list = new Container();

        panel.roundRect(470, 24, 266, 372, 18, 0.6).fill(0x1c1c1e);
        stage.addChildAt(panel, 0);
        stage.addChild(list);
        const style = (fill: number) => ({ fontFamily: 'Inter, system-ui, sans-serif', fontSize: 13, fill });
        const render = () => {
            list.removeChildren().forEach((c) => {
                c.destroy();
            });
            const rows = [
                ...history.past.slice(-8).map((s) => ({ label: s.label, done: true })),
                ...history.future.slice(0, 4).map((s) => ({ label: s.label, done: false })),
            ];
            const title = new Text({ text: 'History', style: style(0x8e8e93), resolution: 4 });

            title.position.set(494, 42);
            list.addChild(title);
            if (!rows.length) rows.push({ label: 'Nothing yet', done: false });
            rows.forEach((row, i) => {
                const t = new Text({ text: row.label, style: style(row.done ? 0xf2f2f7 : 0x636366), resolution: 4 });

                t.position.set(494, 74 + i * 26);
                list.addChild(t);
            });
        };

        stops.push(history.onChange(render));
        render();
        selection.pick(items[1]);

        return () => {
            for (const stop of stops) stop();
        };
    },
});
```

### Selection

`Selection` holds what an editor has selected. It keeps the items in the order they were picked,
the **primary** item (the last one picked), and the item under the pointer. All three are
signals, so anything can follow them.

```ts

declare const layers: Container[];

const selection = new Selection<Container>();

for (const layer of layers) {
    layer.on('pointerdown', (e) => selection.pick(layer, e.shiftKey ? 'toggle' : 'replace'));
    layer.on('pointerover', () => selection.state.hovered.set(layer));
}
selection.onChange(({ added, removed }) => console.log(added, removed));
```

- `add`, `remove`, `toggle`, `replace` and `clear` change the set. `pick` does what a click does.
- `selectWhere(pool, test)` selects by a test, such as every layer inside a marquee.
- `focus(item)` makes a selected item primary without changing the set.
- `filter` keeps locked layers out, and `multiple: false` keeps one item at most.

The store works for any item type: containers, ids or your own records.

#### One call per widget

Adapters make the widgets follow the selection. Each returns the function that stops it.

```ts

declare const selection: Selection<Container>;
declare const outline: SelectionOutline, hover: SelectionOutline;
declare const gizmo: TransformGizmo, measure: Measure;

bindOutline(selection, outline, hover);
bindGizmo(selection, gizmo);
bindMeasure(selection, measure);
```

`bindOutline` frames the selection, and the second outline shows the hovered item. `bindGizmo`
attaches the gizmo to the primary item. `bindMeasure` shows its size and the distances to the
hovered item. The selection module draws nothing itself, so it adds about 1 kB.

### Undo and redo

`History` is an undo stack of commands. A command can `do()` and `undo()` a change.

```ts

declare const layer: { name: string };

const history = new History();

history.execute({ label: 'Rename', do: () => (layer.name = 'Hero'), undo: () => (layer.name = 'Layer 1') });
history.undo();
history.redo();
```

- `record(command)` stores a change that already happened, such as the end of a drag.
- `begin()` and `end()` make everything in between one step, so a whole drag undoes at once.
  `transaction(label, fn)` does both, and reverts the changes if `fn` throws.
- A command with `merge()` can fold the next one into itself, so typing or nudges become one step.
- A new change drops the steps you could redo. `maxDepth` caps the stack, 200 by default.
- `state.canUndo`, `state.canRedo` and the labels are signals for buttons and menus.

`bindHistoryKeys(history)` binds cmd or ctrl + Z to undo, and Shift + cmd + Z and ctrl + Y to
redo. Keys typed into inputs and text areas stay with them.

#### Undo without commands

`recordSignals` records changes of a set of signals. You keep writing to the signals, and
undo works.

```ts

const history = new History();
const doc = { title: signal('Untitled'), zoom: signal(1) };

recordSignals(history, doc);
doc.title.value = 'Poster';
history.undo();
```

Changes made in one handler become one step. Inside a transaction they merge into its step. The
default `patch` mode stores only the signals that changed. `snapshot` stores all of them, for
values that depend on each other.

#### Your own store

Widgets take any `HistoryLike`: an object with `record`, `begin` and `end`. Give them an adapter
over your own state library or patch log, and the same edits land there.

```ts

declare const store: { push(entry: { label?: string; undo(): void; redo(): void }): void };

const history: HistoryLike = {
    record: (c) => store.push({ label: c.label, undo: () => c.undo(), redo: () => c.do() }),
    begin: () => {},
    end: () => {},
};
```

#### Gizmo drags

`recordGizmo(gizmo, history)` turns each drag of a `TransformGizmo` into one step, labelled Move,
Rotate, Scale or Resize. It watches the gizmo's `dragging` signal, so the gizmo does not depend
on history.

```ts

declare const gizmo: TransformGizmo;

const history = new History();

recordGizmo(gizmo, history);
bindHistoryKeys(history);
```

The canvas demo on [Canvas and measurements](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/canvas-and-measurements/) uses the same
selection and history.

---

# Path editing

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/path-editing/
Area: Editor UI

> Edit bezier paths in PixiJS like a design tool: drag anchors and handles, add and delete points, and draw with a pen tool. Every edit is undoable.

Demo sources run in the docs harness, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `path-editing` (source):

```ts
import { Container, Text } from 'pixi.js';
import { linear, SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { bindHistoryKeys, History } from 'pixi-silk/history';
import { type BezierPath, drawPath, parseSvgPath, pathBounds } from 'pixi-silk/path';
import { watch } from 'pixi-silk/signal';
import { SilkButton } from 'pixi-silk/ui/button';
import { Camera } from 'pixi-silk/ui/camera';
import { SegmentedSwitch } from 'pixi-silk/ui/modes';
import { PathGizmo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/pathgizmo';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Drag anchors and handles. Click the outline to add a point, double-click a point to switch it
// between corner and smooth, Alt + drag a handle to break the curve. The pen adds points: click
// for corners, drag for curves, click the first point to close. Cmd or ctrl + Z undoes.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [760, 480],
    background: 0x151517,
    touchAction: 'none',
    ownsCamera: true,
    controls: {
        snap: { type: 'toggle', label: 'snap to 10', value: false },
    },
    setup({ silk, params }) {
        const app = silk.app;
        const world = new Container();
        const overlay = new Container();
        const camera = new Camera({ world, element: app.canvas });
        const history = new History();
        const shape = new SilkGraphics();
        const [heart] = parseSvgPath(
            'M0 -60 C 0 -140 -140 -140 -140 -40 C -140 60 0 120 0 180 C 0 120 140 60 140 -40 C 140 -140 0 -140 0 -60 Z',
        );
        const gizmo = new PathGizmo({ path: heart, space: world, history, ticker: app.ticker });
        const paint = linear([0xff375f, 0xbf5af2], { from: [0, 0], to: [1, 1] });

        world.addChild(shape);
        overlay.addChild(gizmo);
        app.stage.addChild(world, overlay);
        gizmo.snap = (p) => (params.snap ? { x: Math.round(p.x / 10) * 10, y: Math.round(p.y / 10) * 10 } : p);

        // the shape is drawn from the path: one exact fill, then the stroke
        const draw = (path: BezierPath) => {
            shape.clear();
            if (path.anchors.length < 2) return;
            if (path.closed) drawPath(shape, path).fill(paint);
            drawPath(shape, path).stroke({ width: 2, color: 0xf2f2f7, alpha: path.closed ? 0.35 : 0.9 });
        };
        const stops = [watch(gizmo.state.path, draw)];

        // a toolbar: tools, undo, redo and a fresh path for the pen
        const dark = { fill: 0x2c2c2e, fillHover: 0x3a3a3c, fillPress: 0x1c1c1e, fillDisabled: 0x232325 };
        const tools = new SegmentedSwitch({
            ticker: app.ticker,
            value: 'edit',
            segmentWidth: 64,
            height: 32,
            options: [
                { id: 'edit', label: 'Edit' },
                { id: 'pen', label: 'Pen' },
            ],
        });
        const button = (label: string, onPress: () => void) =>
            new SilkButton({ ticker: app.ticker, label, width: 72, height: 32, theme: dark, onPress });
        const undo = button('Undo', () => history.undo());
        const redo = button('Redo', () => history.redo());
        const fresh = button('New', () => {
            gizmo.edit('New path', () => ({ anchors: [], closed: false }), []);
            gizmo.state.tool.set('pen');
        });
        const hint = new Text({
            text: '',
            style: { fontFamily: 'Inter, system-ui, sans-serif', fontSize: 12, fill: 0x8e8e93 },
            resolution: 4,
        });

        overlay.addChild(tools, undo, redo, fresh, hint);
        tools.position.set(76, 28);
        undo.position.set(200, 28);
        redo.position.set(280, 28);
        fresh.position.set(360, 28);
        hint.position.set(16, 452);
        stops.push(
            // the switch and the gizmo share one tool
            watch(tools.state.value, (v) => gizmo.state.tool.set(v === 'pen' ? 'pen' : 'edit')),
            watch(gizmo.state.tool, (v) => tools.state.value.set(v)),
            watch(history.state.canUndo, (v) => undo.state.enabled.set(v)),
            watch(history.state.canRedo, (v) => redo.state.enabled.set(v)),
            watch(history.state.undoLabel, (label) => {
                hint.text = label
                    ? `Undo ${label.toLowerCase()} with cmd or ctrl + Z`
                    : 'Edit the heart, or press New to draw';
            }),
            bindHistoryKeys(history),
        );

        const b = pathBounds(heart);

        camera.fit(b, 72, false);

        return () => {
            for (const stop of stops) stop();
            camera.destroy();
        };
    },
});
```

### Paths as data

A path is plain data: anchors in order and a closed flag. Each anchor has a position, optional
`in` and `out` handles (offsets from the anchor) and a type.

- `corner`: each handle moves on its own. Without handles it is a sharp corner.
- `smooth`: the handles stay on one line, each with its own length.
- `mirrored`: the handles stay on one line and have the same length.

```ts

const drop: BezierPath = {
    closed: true,
    anchors: [
        { x: 0, y: -80, type: 'corner' },
        { x: 50, y: 20, in: { x: 0, y: -40 }, out: { x: 0, y: 40 }, type: 'smooth' },
        { x: -50, y: 20, in: { x: 0, y: 40 }, out: { x: 0, y: -40 }, type: 'smooth' },
    ],
};
```

It fits in JSON, in a signal and in an undo step as it is. The helpers in `pixi-silk/path`
never change their input. Each one returns a new path.

`drawPath(g, path)` draws it into a `SilkGraphics` with `moveTo`, `bezierCurveTo` and `closePath`.
The fill is one exact shape, so it stays sharp at any zoom.

```ts

declare const drop: BezierPath;

const g = new SilkGraphics();

drawPath(g, drop).fill(linear([0x64d2ff, 0x0a84ff])).stroke({ width: 2, color: 0xffffff });
```

#### Editing helpers

- `insertAnchor(path, segment, t)` splits a segment with de Casteljau's construction. The curve
  keeps its exact shape.
- `removeAnchors`, `moveAnchors` and `setHandle` edit points. `setHandle` keeps the twin handle in
  line for smooth and mirrored anchors.
- `setAnchorType` and `toggleAnchorType` switch between corners and curves. A corner without
  handles grows them from its neighbours.
- `nearestOnPath`, `pathBounds`, `splitCubic` and `anchorsInRect` measure and hit-test.
- `parseSvgPath` reads SVG path data, arcs and quadratics included. `pathToSvg` writes it back.
  `mapPath` moves a path through any affine map.

### The path gizmo

`PathGizmo` edits a path on screen. Put it in an overlay and set `space` to the world the path
lives in. Anchors and handles then keep their size on screen at every zoom.

```ts

declare const world: Container, overlay: Container, drop: BezierPath;

const history = new History();
const gizmo = new PathGizmo({ path: drop, space: world, history });
const shape = new SilkGraphics();

world.addChild(shape);
overlay.addChild(gizmo);
watch(gizmo.state.path, (path) => drawPath(shape.clear(), path).fill(0x0a84ff));
```

- Click an anchor to select it and Shift + click to add more. Drag to move them all.
- Drag a handle to bend the curve. Alt + drag breaks the symmetry.
- Double-click an anchor to switch between a corner and a smooth point.
- Click a segment to add an anchor there. The outline lights up under the pointer first.
- Delete or Backspace removes the selected anchors. Arrow keys nudge them, Shift by 10.

`selectInRect(rect)` selects the anchors inside a rectangle, the hook for a marquee. `snap`
moves dragged points, for a grid or for smart guides. `setClosed` closes or opens the path.

#### Pen tool

Set `state.tool` to `pen` to draw. Click for a corner, drag for a curve with mirrored handles.
Alt during a drag moves only the outgoing handle. Click the last anchor to drop its outgoing
handle, and click the first one to close the path. Escape or Enter stops drawing.

`PenTool` is the pen's state machine on its own, with no pointer events or drawing. Feed it
presses, moves and releases to build paths from any input.

#### Undo

With a `history`, every edit is one undo step: a whole drag, an inserted point, a pen click, a
run of nudges. The gizmo needs only the `HistoryLike` interface, so your own store works too. See
[Selection and undo](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/selection-and-history/).

---

# Colour pickers

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/color-pickers/
Area: Editor UI

> Hue ring with triangle or square, colour wheel, OKLCH plane, channel sliders, swatches, a radial swatch popup and an eyedropper, all on one colour model.

Demo sources run in the docs harness, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `color-rings` (source):

```ts
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { ColorModel, ColorWheel, HueSquarePicker, HueTrianglePicker, SwatchPalette } from 'pixi-silk/ui/color';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Three pickers bound to one model: drag any of them and the others follow. Releasing a drag
// commits the colour into the recent row at the bottom.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [740, 330],
    touchAction: 'none',
    setup({ silk, stage, tick }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const model = new ColorModel({ color: 0x30d158 });
        const triangle = new HueTrianglePicker({ model, size: 220, ticker });
        const square = new HueSquarePicker({ model, size: 220, ticker });
        const wheel = new ColorWheel({ model, size: 196, ticker });
        const recent = new SwatchPalette({ model, columns: 12, size: 26, gap: 8, ticker });
        const chip = new SilkGraphics();

        triangle.position.set(20, 16);
        square.position.set(262, 16);
        wheel.position.set(506, 28);
        recent.position.set(64, 278);
        chip.position.set(20, 278);
        stage.addChild(triangle, square, wheel, recent, chip);
        for (const c of [0xff453a, 0xffd60a, 0x0a84ff]) {
            model.setHex(c);
            model.commit();
        }
        model.setHex(0x30d158);

        tick(() => {
            chip.clear().roundRect(0, 0, 32, 26, 8).fill(model.hex);
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

### One model, many pickers

Every picker edits a `ColorModel`. Give several pickers the same model and they stay in sync. The
model stores hue, saturation, value and alpha, so the hue survives greys and black. It also reads
and writes hex, RGB and OKLCH.

```ts

const model = new ColorModel({ color: 0x30d158 });
const triangle = new HueTrianglePicker({ model, size: 220 });
const wheel = new ColorWheel({ model, size: 196 });
const recent = new SwatchPalette({ model }); // no colours given: shows the recent ones

declare const brush: { tint: number };
declare function save(color: number): void;

model.value.subscribe(() => (brush.tint = model.hex)); // live, while dragging
model.committed.subscribe(() => save(model.hex)); // once per release
```

`value` changes on every move of a drag. Pickers call `commit()` when a drag ends, which adds the
colour to `history` and bumps `committed`.

### The pickers

- **`HueTrianglePicker`**: hue ring around a triangle with the pure hue, white and black at its
  corners. The triangle turns to face its hue.
- **`HueSquarePicker`**: hue ring around a saturation and value square.
- **`ColorWheel`**: hue around a disc, saturation from the centre out, value on a strip beside it.
- **`OklchPicker`**: lightness against chroma for one OKLCH hue, plus a hue strip. Colours keep
  their look as you move, and the part outside sRGB is greyed out.
- **`ColorSlider`** and **`ChannelSliders`**: gradient tracks for HSV, RGB, OKLCH and alpha. Each
  track shows what its positions would give with the rest of the colour unchanged.
- **`SwatchPalette`**: fixed colours or the recent ones, as a grid or a ring.
- **`SwatchPopup`**: a ring of swatches that opens around the pointer.
- **`Eyedropper`**: samples the scene through a function the app supplies.

A drag belongs to the part it started on. Start on the ring and the drag only turns the hue, even
across the square. Handles grow under a finger and glide when another picker moves the colour.

Live demo `color-oklch` (source):

```ts
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { ChannelSliders, ColorModel, checker, OklchPicker } from 'pixi-silk/ui/color';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Lightness against chroma for one OKLCH hue. The grey area is outside sRGB; dragging into it
// slides along the edge. The sliders edit the same colour in the chosen space.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [600, 260],
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        sliders: { type: 'select', label: 'sliders', options: ['oklch', 'hsv', 'rgb'], value: 'oklch' },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const model = new ColorModel({ color: 0xbf5af2 });
        const plane = new OklchPicker({ model, width: 260, height: 180, ticker });
        const preview = new SilkGraphics();
        let mode = '';
        let sliders: ChannelSliders | null = null;

        plane.position.set(20, 20);
        preview.position.set(330, 196);
        stage.addChild(plane, preview);

        tick(() => {
            if (params.sliders !== mode) {
                mode = params.sliders;
                sliders?.destroy();
                sliders = new ChannelSliders({ model, mode: params.sliders as 'oklch', width: 220, ticker });
                sliders.position.set(330, 24);
                stage.addChild(sliders);
            }
            preview.clear();
            checker(preview, 22, 0, 220, 36, 10, 9);
            preview.roundRect(22, 0, 220, 36, 10).fill({ color: model.hex, alpha: model.alpha });
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

### OKLCH

OKLCH is lightness, chroma and hue in a space built to match how colours look. Moving lightness
does not shift the hue, and equal steps look equal. Not every OKLCH colour fits on a screen, so the
picker greys out the area outside sRGB and draws its edge. A drag into that area slides along the
edge.

```ts

const model = new ColorModel();
const plane = new OklchPicker({ model, width: 260, height: 180 });
const sliders = new ChannelSliders({ model, mode: 'oklch' }); // 'hsv', 'rgb'; alpha on by default
```

Live demo `color-popup` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { horizontal, SilkGraphics, vertical } from 'pixi-silk';
import { SilkButton } from 'pixi-silk/ui/button';
import { ColorModel, Eyedropper, hsvToRgb, rgbToHex, SwatchPalette, SwatchPopup } from 'pixi-silk/ui/color';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Press anywhere on the artwork for the swatch ring: drag to a colour and let go, or tap to keep
// it open. The pipette samples the artwork (the app supplies the sampler, here plain maths).
const W = 600;
const H = 260;
const ART = { x: 20, y: 20, w: 420, h: 220 };
/** Colour of the artwork at a design-space point: hue across, value down. */
const artColor = (x: number, y: number): number | null => {
    const u = (x - ART.x) / ART.w;
    const v = (y - ART.y) / ART.h;

    return u < 0 || u > 1 || v < 0 || v > 1 ? null : rgbToHex(hsvToRgb(u * 360, 0.85, 1 - v * 0.8));
};
const pipette = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    g.line(-s * 0.32, s * 0.32, s * 0.12, -s * 0.12).stroke({ width: s * 0.16, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
    g.circle(s * 0.22, -s * 0.22, s * 0.16).fill(0xffffff);
};

export default defineDemo({
    size: [W, H],
    touchAction: 'none',
    setup({ silk, stage }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const model = new ColorModel({ color: 0xff9f0a });
        const art = new SilkGraphics();
        const surface = new Container();
        const popup = new SwatchPopup({
            model,
            colors: [0xff453a, 0xff9f0a, 0xffd60a, 0x30d158, 0x64d2ff, 0x0a84ff, 0xbf5af2, 0xffffff],
            ticker,
        });
        const chip = new SilkGraphics();
        const recent = new SwatchPalette({ model, columns: 4, size: 26, gap: 8, ticker });
        const dropper = new Eyedropper({
            model,
            ticker,
            // global canvas point -> design space of the demo
            sample: (gx, gy) => {
                const p = stage.toLocal({ x: gx, y: gy });

                return artColor(p.x, p.y);
            },
        });
        const button = new SilkButton({
            shape: 'circle',
            height: 44,
            icon: pipette,
            ticker,
            theme: { fill: 0x2c2c2e, fillHover: 0x3a3a3c, fillPress: 0x1c1c1e },
            onPress: () => dropper.start(),
        });

        art.roundRect(ART.x, ART.y, ART.w, ART.h, 14).fill({
            gradient: horizontal([0xff0000, 0xffff00, 0x00ff00, 0x00ffff, 0x0000ff, 0xff00ff, 0xff0000], {
                space: 'srgb',
            }),
        });
        art.roundRect(ART.x, ART.y, ART.w, ART.h, 14).fill({ color: 0xffffff, alpha: 0.15 });
        art.roundRect(ART.x, ART.y, ART.w, ART.h, 14).fill({
            gradient: vertical(
                [
                    [0, 0x000000, 0],
                    [1, 0x000000, 0.8],
                ],
                { space: 'srgb' },
            ),
        });
        surface.eventMode = 'static';
        surface.hitArea = { contains: (x, y) => x >= ART.x && y >= ART.y && x <= ART.x + ART.w && y <= ART.y + ART.h };
        surface.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
            const p = stage.toLocal(e.global);

            popup.openAt(p.x, p.y, e);
        });
        button.position.set(482, 42);
        chip.position.set(520, 20);
        recent.position.set(462, 96);
        stage.addChild(art, surface, chip, recent, button, popup, dropper);
        model.value.subscribe(() => chip.clear().roundRect(0, 0, 60, 44, 12).fill(model.hex));
        chip.roundRect(0, 0, 60, 44, 12).fill(model.hex);

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

### Swatch ring and eyedropper

The swatch popup works like the [radial menu](https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/radial-menus-and-buttons/). Press, drag towards
a colour and let go, or tap to keep it open.

The eyedropper does not read pixels itself. Your app knows its scene, so it supplies a sampler.
That can be a texture lookup, `renderer.extract` on a render target, or plain maths.

```ts

declare const canvasArea: Container;
declare const pipetteButton: Container;
declare function colorAt(x: number, y: number): number | null;

const model = new ColorModel();
const popup = new SwatchPopup({ model, colors: [0xff453a, 0xffd60a, 0x30d158, 0x0a84ff] });
const dropper = new Eyedropper({ model, sample: (x, y) => colorAt(x, y) });

canvasArea.on('pointerdown', (e) => popup.openAt(e.global.x, e.global.y, e));
pipetteButton.on('pointertap', () => dropper.start()); // click to pick, Escape or right click to cancel
```

All pickers draw with pixi-silk gradients. Hue rings are conic gradients interpolated in sRGB, so
every angle is an exact HSV hue. The square and the wheel layer white and black ramps for exact
HSV colours. The OKLCH plane is drawn row by row, and chroma along a row is a straight line in
OKLab, so every row is exact too.

---

# Editor tools

URL: https://pixi-silk.schmooky.dev/docs/editor-tools/
Area: Editor UI

> Action buttons that orbit the selection, a brush cursor with live resize and pen pressure, a hold-to-show mode ring and a sliding segmented switch.

Demo sources run in the docs harness, `defineDemo({ setup({ stage, params, tick }) })`. In your own app, `stage` is `app.stage` (or a container on it), `params` holds the values of the demo controls, and `tick(fn)` is `app.ticker.add`, with `t` and `dt` in seconds.

Live demo `halo` (source):

```ts
import { Container } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { trackGesture } from 'pixi-silk/ui/gesture';
import { ContextHalo } from 'pixi-silk/ui/halo';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Icons drawn in white around (0, 0). The halo tints them.
const copy = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    g.roundRect(-s * 0.4, -s * 0.2, s * 0.55, s * 0.6, 3).stroke({ width: s * 0.1, color: 0xffffff });
    g.roundRect(-s * 0.15, -s * 0.45, s * 0.55, s * 0.6, 3).fill(0xffffff);
};
const trash = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    g.roundRect(-s * 0.3, -s * 0.22, s * 0.6, s * 0.66, 3).fill(0xffffff);
    g.line(-s * 0.42, -s * 0.34, s * 0.42, -s * 0.34).stroke({ width: s * 0.1, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
};
const lock = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    g.roundRect(-s * 0.32, -s * 0.05, s * 0.64, s * 0.46, 4).fill(0xffffff);
    g.arcSweep(0, -s * 0.08, s * 0.2, Math.PI, Math.PI).stroke({ width: s * 0.1, color: 0xffffff });
};
const front = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => {
    g.regularPoly(0, -s * 0.12, s * 0.3, 3, 0, 2).fill(0xffffff);
    g.line(-s * 0.3, s * 0.32, s * 0.3, s * 0.32).stroke({ width: s * 0.1, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
};
const paint = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => g.circle(0, 0, s * 0.36).fill(0xffffff);

// Click a shape to select it. Drag it around: the halo follows and turns away from the edges.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [640, 380],
    touchAction: 'none',
    controls: {
        hideWhileDragging: { type: 'toggle', label: 'hide while dragging', value: false },
    },
    setup({ silk, stage, params, tick }) {
        const world = new Container();
        const halo = new ContextHalo({
            items: [
                { id: 'copy', label: 'Duplicate', icon: copy },
                { id: 'paint', label: 'Colour', icon: paint },
                { id: 'front', label: 'Bring to front', icon: front },
                { id: 'lock', label: 'Lock', icon: lock, disabled: true },
                { id: 'delete', label: 'Delete', icon: trash },
            ],
            bounds: { x: 0, y: 0, width: 640, height: 380 },
            ticker: silk.app.ticker,
            onSelect: (item) => {
                const target = halo.state.target.value as SilkGraphics | null;

                if (!target) return;
                if (item.id === 'delete') {
                    halo.detach();
                    target.destroy();
                } else if (item.id === 'front') world.addChild(target);
                else if (item.id === 'paint') redraw(target, (Math.random() * 0xffffff) | 0);
            },
        });
        const redraw = (g: SilkGraphics, colour: number) => {
            g.clear().roundRect(-60, -40, 120, 80, 18, 0.6).fill(colour);
        };
        const backdrop = new Container();

        backdrop.eventMode = 'static';
        backdrop.hitArea = { contains: () => true };
        backdrop.on('pointerdown', () => halo.detach());
        stage.addChild(backdrop, world, halo);

        [
            [170, 190, 0x0a84ff],
            [420, 110, 0x30d158],
            [520, 300, 0xff9f0a],
        ].forEach(([x, y, colour]) => {
            const g = new SilkGraphics();
            let grab = { x: 0, y: 0 };

            redraw(g, colour);
            g.position.set(x, y);
            g.cursor = 'grab';
            world.addChild(g);
            trackGesture(g, {
                onDown: (p) => {
                    halo.attach(g);
                    grab = { x: p.x - g.x, y: p.y - g.y };
                },
                onDragStart: () => halo.state.dragging.set(true),
                onDrag: (p) => g.position.set(p.x - grab.x, p.y - grab.y),
                onDragEnd: () => halo.state.dragging.set(false),
            });
        });
        halo.attach(world.children[0]);

        tick(() => {
            halo.hideWhileDragging = params.hideWhileDragging;
        });

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

Each tool has its own entry point and pulls in nothing else:

```ts
```

### Context halo

Round action buttons orbit the selected object, the way 3D and design tools show context actions.

```ts

const halo = new ContextHalo({
    items: [
        { id: 'copy', label: 'Duplicate', icon: drawMove },
        { id: 'delete', label: 'Delete', icon: drawTrash },
    ],
    bounds: { x: 0, y: 0, width: app.screen.width, height: app.screen.height },
    onSelect: (item) => console.log(item.id, halo.state.target.value),
});

app.stage.addChild(halo);
halo.attach(stage.children[0]); // the selected object
halo.state.dragging.set(true); // while the user drags the selection
```

- The buttons follow the target's bounds every frame, so drags, tweens and camera moves need no wiring.
- Selecting another object never slides the buttons across the scene. They pop out at the old
  object and pop back in around the new one, with a stagger. `transition: 'glide'` slides instead.
  Input is off while they pop.
- They sit on an arc that hugs the target's box, evenly spaced on wide and tall shapes alike.
- Near an edge of `bounds` the arc turns to the side with room, gliding round rather than jumping.
- While `state.dragging` is true they follow faster, or fold away with `hideWhileDragging`.
- A label pill shows on hover. Disabled items dim and ignore taps.

### Brush cursor

Live demo `brush` (source):

```ts
import { Container, RenderTexture, Sprite } from 'pixi.js';
import { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { BrushCursor } from 'pixi-silk/ui/brush';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

// Paint on the canvas. Hold F and move (or drag with the right button) to resize: sideways is
// size, up and down is hardness. [ and ] step the size. A pen's pressure shows as the inner disc.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [640, 360],
    touchAction: 'none',
    setup({ silk, stage, width, height }) {
        const surface = new Container();
        const canvas = RenderTexture.create({ width, height, resolution: silk.resolution });
        const dab = new SilkGraphics();
        const brush = new BrushCursor({ size: 36, hardness: 0.6, surface, ticker: silk.app.ticker });
        let painting = false;
        let last: { x: number; y: number } | null = null;

        surface.eventMode = 'static';
        surface.hitArea = { contains: (x, y) => x >= 0 && y >= 0 && x <= width && y <= height };
        stage.addChild(new Sprite(canvas), surface, brush);

        const stamp = (x: number, y: number, pressure: number) => {
            const { size, hardness, opacity } = brush.state;
            const r = (size.value / 2) * (0.35 + 0.65 * pressure);

            // soft brushes are a blurred dab, hard ones a crisp disc
            dab.clear()
                .circle(x, y, r * (0.6 + 0.4 * hardness.value))
                .fill({ color: 0x0a84ff, alpha: 0.35 * opacity.value, blur: r * (1 - hardness.value) * 0.6 });
            silk.app.renderer.render({ container: dab, target: canvas, clear: false });
        };
        const paintTo = (x: number, y: number, pressure: number) => {
            const from = last ?? { x, y };
            const step = Math.max(1, brush.state.size.value * 0.12);
            const n = Math.max(1, Math.ceil(Math.hypot(x - from.x, y - from.y) / step));

            for (let i = 1; i <= n; i++)
                stamp(from.x + ((x - from.x) * i) / n, from.y + ((y - from.y) * i) / n, pressure);
            last = { x, y };
        };

        surface.on('pointerdown', (e) => {
            if (e.button !== 0 || brush.state.resizing.value) return;
            painting = true;
            last = null;
            const p = stage.toLocal(e.global);

            paintTo(p.x, p.y, e.pointerType === 'pen' ? e.pressure : 0.6);
        });
        surface.on('globalpointermove', (e) => {
            if (!painting || brush.state.resizing.value) return;
            const p = stage.toLocal(e.global);

            paintTo(p.x, p.y, e.pointerType === 'pen' ? e.pressure : 0.6);
        });
        const stop = () => {
            painting = false;
            last = null;
        };

        surface.on('pointerup', stop);
        surface.on('pointerupoutside', stop);

        return () => canvas.destroy(true);
    },
});
```

```ts

const brush = new BrushCursor({ size: 24, hardness: 0.8, surface: stage });

app.stage.addChild(brush);
brush.state.size.subscribe((size) => console.log('brush size', size));
```

- The ring is the brush size. A dashed inner ring shows hardness, and a disc grows with pen pressure.
- Small brushes get a crosshair, so the hot spot stays visible.
- Hold `F` (`resizeKey`) or drag with the right button to resize. The ring stays where the resize
  began, and a pill reads out size and hardness.
- `[` and `]` step the size. The OS cursor is hidden over `surface`.

### Mode switcher

Live demo `modes` (source):

```ts
import { Text } from 'pixi.js';
import type { SilkGraphics } from 'pixi-silk';
import { ModeSwitcher, SegmentedSwitch } from 'pixi-silk/ui/modes';
import { defineDemo } from './runtime';

type Icon = (g: SilkGraphics, s: number) => void;
const brush: Icon = (g, s) => {
    g.line(-s * 0.3, s * 0.3, s * 0.3, -s * 0.3).stroke({ width: s * 0.14, cap: 'round', color: 0xffffff });
    g.circle(-s * 0.32, s * 0.32, s * 0.14).fill(0xffffff);
};
const eraser: Icon = (g, s) => g.roundRect(-s * 0.36, -s * 0.2, s * 0.72, s * 0.4, 4).fill(0xffffff);
const smudge: Icon = (g, s) => g.heart(0, 0, s * 0.7, 0.2).fill(0xffffff);
const fill: Icon = (g, s) => g.regularPoly(0, 0, s * 0.38, 6, 0, 3).fill(0xffffff);
const pick: Icon = (g, s) => {
    g.circle(0, 0, s * 0.3).stroke({ width: s * 0.1, color: 0xffffff });
    g.circle(0, 0, s * 0.08).fill(0xffffff);
};

// Hold Space over the canvas (or long-press it) for the mode ring, release on a mode.
// Tap Space to swap back to the previous mode. The pill below is a SegmentedSwitch.
export default defineDemo({
    size: [640, 360],
    touchAction: 'none',
    setup({ silk, stage, width, height }) {
        const ticker = silk.app.ticker;
        const status = new Text({
            text: '',
            style: { fill: 0xf2f2f7, fontFamily: 'system-ui, sans-serif', fontSize: 18, fontWeight: '600' },
        });
        const hint = new Text({
            text: 'hold Space or long-press, tap Space to swap back',
            style: { fill: 0x8e8e93, fontFamily: 'system-ui, sans-serif', fontSize: 13 },
        });
        const speed = new SegmentedSwitch({
            options: [
                { id: 'slow', label: 'Slow' },
                { id: 'normal', label: 'Normal' },
                { id: 'turbo', label: 'Turbo' },
            ],
            value: 'normal',
            ticker,
        });
        const tools = new SegmentedSwitch({
            options: [
                { id: 'brush', icon: brush },
                { id: 'eraser', icon: eraser },
                { id: 'smudge', icon: smudge },
                { id: 'fill', icon: fill },
                { id: 'pick', icon: pick },
            ],
            segmentWidth: 48,
            ticker,
        });
        const modes = new ModeSwitcher({
            modes: [
                { id: 'brush', label: 'BRUSH', icon: brush },
                { id: 'eraser', label: 'ERASER', icon: eraser },
                { id: 'smudge', label: 'SMUDGE', icon: smudge },
                { id: 'fill', label: 'FILL', icon: fill },
                { id: 'pick', label: 'PICKER', icon: pick },
            ],
            longPressTarget: stage,
            ticker,
        });

        stage.eventMode = 'static';
        stage.hitArea = { contains: (x, y) => x >= 0 && y >= 0 && x <= width && y <= height };
        status.anchor.set(0.5);
        hint.anchor.set(0.5);
        status.position.set(width / 2, 70);
        hint.position.set(width / 2, 100);
        tools.position.set(width / 2, 190);
        speed.position.set(width / 2, 290);
        stage.addChild(status, hint, tools, speed, modes);

        // the switcher and the toolbar share one source of truth
        const show = (mode: string) => {
            status.text = `mode: ${mode}`;
            tools.state.value.set(mode);
        };

        modes.state.mode.subscribe(show);
        tools.state.value.subscribe((id) => modes.set(id));
        show(modes.mode);

        return undefined;
    },
});
```

```ts

const modes = new ModeSwitcher({
    modes: [
        { id: 'brush', label: 'BRUSH', icon: drawPointer },
        { id: 'eraser', label: 'ERASER', icon: drawTrash },
    ],
    longPressTarget: app.stage,
    onChange: (mode, previous) => console.log(previous, '->', mode),
});

app.stage.addChild(modes);
```
